# Cellarium — backlog

The single authoritative task list for Cellarium. Organized by **task class** (A–H); within each class, by
**priority**. **New tasks, findings, and bugs go here** — under the right class, with an ID, priority, and a
one-line description; do not spin up separate audit/TODO docs.

**Scope.** These are our items (Evangelos's audit + roadmap + TODO). Filippo's Council-defect ledger (D1–D6, on the
`operationalization-debate` branch) is a **separate, Filippo-owned** workstream; it is *cross-referenced* where it
touches an item here, never folded in.

**Priority.** `P1` — before publication / open-source. `P2` — soon. `P3` — polish / later.
**Source.** `A` audit · `T` TODO · `R` roadmap/old-audit open items · `N` new (surfaced in reconciliation) · `S`
AI-for-Science direction. Audit IDs (M-/DS-/LLM-/AG-/D-/UX-/H-/SP-) carry over from the 2026-07-14 audit; its full
file:line evidence lives in git history (commit `55ed67f`).

**▶ Next action:** the **live PUB-A1 sweep** — the last step in the publication's headline comparison, and the only
one that needs an API key. Everything offline is built and green. See
[⏳ TODO — the live PUB-A1 sweep](#-todo--the-live-pub-a1-sweep-the-last-step-that-needs-a-key).

> ⚠️ **Ordering caveat (literature-backed, `wf_5cb5c805`): do PUB-A2 first, or at least in the same sitting.**
> **Validity is bounded by reliability** — buying tighter error bars on an *unvalidated judge axis* spends budget
> on precision we cannot interpret. Miller, *Adding Error Bars to Evals* (arXiv:2411.00640) treats evals as
> experiments requiring planning, not just analysis; and Thakur et al., *Judging the Judges* (arXiv:2406.12624)
> is the settled position that **no LLM-judge endpoint is publishable without a reported agreement statistic**.
> `shared_metric.grade()` already takes `(client, model)` and its payload is arm-blind, so the panel is a loop
> over judges plus κ/α — cheap to add *before* the 140-arm-run spend, expensive to retrofit after.
>
> **Update 2026-07-26 — the ordering is now much less costly to get wrong.** `grade()` returns `graded_text`, so
> the ledger stores the exact artifact each score was computed from: a later panel re-grades a **fixed** set
> rather than re-running the sweep. (Re-running would also re-roll every arm, so the panel's numbers would not be
> comparable to the originals — and an agreement statistic requires raters to score the *same* items.) PUB-A2
> first is still the right order; it is no longer a one-way door.

| ~~**M-10**~~ ✅ (with a correction to its own proposed fix) | **DONE 2026-08-08 — and the fix this entry proposed does not apply to this script.** The entry says the cohort 'is already designated at creation' by `evals/run_ab.py` and that the fix is to select by the ledger's `run_id` set. That is true of a DIFFERENT protocol: `evals/run_ab.py` runs the 25 numbered cases in `evals/cases.py`, keyed `1.1`/`4.2`, and writes `ab_ledger.json`, which is what `aggregate_ab.py` reads. `scripts/ab_score.py` scores five HAND-RUN UI questions the ledger has never seen — MEASURED: **`pfkA` and `argS` appear in no case at all**, while `rRNA` and `ppGpp` match five and six cases respectively. Selecting by that ledger would have silently mis-assigned the cohort, which is a worse version of the defect being fixed. **What actually shipped:** (a) the blindness half is fixed properly — `PRE-REGISTERED (0 corpus reads, blind)` printed a measured-sounding quantity that was NEVER measured, inferred from `status == 'done'` which only means the deliberation finished; it now reads *blind BY CONSTRUCTION*, cites `tests/test_blindness.py`, and says it is a structural invariant rather than a per-run count. Arm A's `corpus_reads` genuinely are counted; arm B has no such field because a Council run has no corpus tools to call. (b) the cohort half is made VISIBLE rather than falsely precise: the real harm was `arm_a[-1]` silently taking the newest of however many matched, so any user-typed question containing the same words scored as a pre-registered arm with nothing saying so. Every question now reports its match count and warns when it is not 1 — live, `lpxC` matches 2 and now says so. **Still open:** this protocol has no cohort registry at all; giving it one means having the UI record which sessions belong to a scored run. *(Original entry follows.)* **A/B cohort selection is by question SUBSTRING, and blindness is asserted from `status`.** `scripts/ab_score.py:74` picks the "Arm B" Council row with `key in r["question"].lower()`, then prints `"PRE-REGISTERED (0 corpus reads, blind)"` from `r["status"] == "done"` alone (`:86`) — a status that only means the deliberation finished. **Any** user-typed question containing the matched substring lands in that selector; no MCP is needed for this to mis-report. The A/B cohort is already **designated at creation** — `evals/run_ab.py:167-168` mints the run id and `:208`/`:323` record it in `evals/results/ab_ledger.json`, which is what `evals/aggregate_ab.py:24-36` actually reads — so the fix is to select by the ledger's `run_id` set, not to add a column. See [`docs/DECISIONS.md` **D10.1**](docs/DECISIONS.md). | A |
| ~~**M-11**~~ ✅ mostly | **CLOSED 2026-08-08 by ARM-1, with ONE deliberate deviation to record.** The ask was "add `kb_sha256` to the `_deduped_rows` projection and make the partition tuple a refusal in the comparison path" — both done. The deviation: the tuple shipped as `(kb_sha256, operons, elongation_model)` WITHOUT `medium`. `condition` is the independent variable in most comparisons, so making it an arm key would refuse the comparisons the corpus exists to make; it is recorded in `corpus_schema.NOT_ARM_KEYS` as a covariate instead. If a medium ever changes what a CHANNEL MEANS rather than what its value is, that decision needs revisiting. Also settled since: the two hashes ARE different knowledge bases (`kb_content_sha256` distinguishes them), and the reference-cell pooling this item predicted was measured — n 4 -> 8 on `wildtype/basal` at depth 4, halving the interval. Original text: **`kb_sha256` is a comparability partition that NOTHING enforces — and it splits the reference design.** MEASURED 2026-08-03 over `data/manifest` with `DEDUP_QUALIFY` applied: the corpus carries **two** `kb_sha256` values (`3b2f8ebd…` 279 rows, `0d861f80…` 43 rows) while `operons='on'` and `elongation_model='steady_state'` are uniform at 322/322. Among `reportable` rows keyed by `survey.design_key`, **2 of 44 design keys mix the two knowledge bases**: `wildtype/basal` (`3b2f8ebd`: 26 seeds, `0d861f80`: 4) and `condition/with_aa` (`3b2f8ebd`: 8, `0d861f80`: 4). `wildtype/basal` is `differential.REFERENCE` (`differential.py:20`) — the reference for **every** comparison. No read path partitions on it: `survey._deduped_rows` (`survey.py:129-162`) does not select `kb_sha256` at all, and `survey.analysis_rows` (`survey.py:178-205`), the single row source for every comparison tool, filters only on `reportable` and `_dropped`. The rule exists **only as prose**: `operons.py:295` ("different knowledge bases … MUST NOT be pooled"), `capability.py:294-295` (a mode switch "changes `kb_sha256`" so the campaign "is not poolable"). Same shape as the dedup incident that inflated this same reference from 26 to 34 seeds (`manifest.py:37-49`), but **semantic, not a dev artifact** — any corpus built from more than one ParCa pass has several hashes, so it does not go away in the clean/production corpus. **Fix:** add `kb_sha256` to the `_deduped_rows` projection and make the partition tuple `(kb_sha256, operons, elongation_model, medium)` a refusal in the comparison path, not a comment. Also NOT ESTABLISHED: whether the two hashes are materially different knowledge bases or one rebuild of the same inputs — `kb_verified` is `True` on 154/322, `False` on 138, NULL on 30, so the corpus cannot currently answer that either. Surfaces as invariant **I2** of [`docs/DECISIONS.md` **D6b-1**](docs/DECISIONS.md). | A |

Fix the destructive re-convene FIRST — it is a standalone win. **Build spec + acceptance tests: [`docs/INVESTIGATION_LOOP.md`](docs/INVESTIGATION_LOOP.md); the thread is `SP-3a…SP-3e` immediately below.** | A |

### D-LOOP · The investigation loop — the build thread (`SP-3`)

*The loop is a **feature**, not a risk. Broad hypotheses → check against simulation results → re-convene with a
sharper question is the working method of research; Cellarium should represent it, not police it. The earlier
position — detect an informed Council and stamp it `unblinded` — is **withdrawn** (`docs/DECISIONS.md` **D6a**
banner + **D10**). What replaces it is a typed lineage, and the reason the lineage matters is stated once here:
**an autoresearch loop whose chain cannot be recovered is a black box that emits confident conclusions.**
Ordered; each row is shippable on its own. **Nothing in this thread ever blocks a round** (M-7 precedent).*

| ID | P | Item | Src |
|----|---|------|-----|
| **SP-3a** | P2 | **Stop the destructive re-convene — the standalone win, do it first.** `run_council(..., reuse_id=X)` sets `run_id = reuse_id` (`apps/hypotheses.py:163`) and calls `store.create`, an `INSERT OR REPLACE` that resets `rounds="[]"` / `hypothesis="{}"` / `designs="[]"` / `meta="{}"` (`apps/hypotheses.py:69-74`) — **a re-convene overwrites the deliberation it was refining.** MEASURED by reading the writer. Consequence: M-7's progressive narrowing (`tests/test_narrowing.py:73-78`, which exercises `reuse_id` twice) leaves **no record of what it narrowed from**, and BACKLOG item (4) below — "round 0 vs round 3 side by side" — is unanswerable because round 0 is gone. Fix: a re-convene INSERTs a new row instead of replacing, and the thread carries the relation. Strictly better than today even if nothing else in `SP-3` ships. | A |
| **SP-3b** | P2 | **The lineage columns + migration.** `council_runs` gains `thread_id TEXT`, `round_index INTEGER`, `informed_by TEXT` (JSON); `sessions` gains `thread_id TEXT`, `from_hyp_id TEXT`. The `ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN` idiom wrapped in `try/except` is already in place at `apps/hypotheses.py:40-43`. **`informed_by` is a TYPED list, never a boolean** — `[{"kind":"investigation","id":…},{"kind":"council_run","id":…},{"kind":"literature","source":…}]` — because blindness in this project is already scoped *by input class*: **literature-informed, corpus-blind** (`docs/HYPOTHESIS_MODE_PLAN.md:32-34`), and `tests/test_blindness.py:19-24` admits `library_brief` at `:23` for exactly that reason. A `blind\|unblinded` field would be **lossier than what the tests already encode**. Blindness then becomes `blindness_of(run_id)` — a **query over the chain** returning the transitive set of input classes — so a later reader can ask a question we did not think to stamp. ⚠️ **Open, NOT ESTABLISHED:** existing rows get `NULL`, which is indistinguishable between *no lineage recorded* and *lineage was empty*; whether to backfill `round_index=0` for pre-D10 rows was not settled (the committed `data/sessions.seed.db` was not opened to count them). | A |
| **SP-3c** | P2 | **Close the Council→investigation edge — stop reconstructing it from prose.** `openInCellwright(run)` holds `run.id` (`apps/web/app.js:1173`) but `send()` posts only `{session_id, question, use_council, model, reasoning}` (`:320`) and `sessions` has no column for it (`apps/sessions.py:35-37`), so today the edge survives **only as the English prefix** *"The Socratic Council framed this falsifiable hypothesis blind to the data…"* (`app.js:1180`) — which `scripts/ab_score.py:42,73` then **substring-matches** to exclude Arm A. Same fragility as `M-10`, in a second place. Three touch points: the POST body (`app.js:320`), the handler (`apps/server.py:169-256`), the INSERT (`apps/sessions.py:72-78`). **Also unblocks `SP-1b`**, whose own text says it "needs a session↔hypothesis link" — though whether `SP-1b`'s agent-side write belongs in the same change was not assessed. | A |
| **SP-3d** | P2 | **The surface — and the one button that IS the feature.** `GET /api/thread?id=` (new route beside `apps/server.py:605-617`) returns the ordered chain; `hypothesis_get` (`apps/server.py:301`) carries `thread_id`/`round_index`/`informed_by` and the detail pane already renders per-design lifecycle at `:304-328`, so the review pane has a home; the run list groups by thread (`apps/hypotheses.py:111-117`) — *round 2 of 3*. **The button: "Re-convene the Council with what Cellwright found"**, on an investigation, minting round N+1 with `informed_by` pre-filled. This is the **return leg**, which today is reachable only by copy-paste — and copy-paste is precisely the route that leaves no lineage. **Lineage is displayed, never enforced.** | A |
| **SP-3e** | P2 | **Keep the A/B evidence base measurable alongside the loop.** It is already insulated — the cohort is designated **at creation** (`evals/run_ab.py:167-168`, `:208`, `:323` → `evals/results/ab_ledger.json`) and `evals/aggregate_ab.py:24-36` reads that ledger, not `council_runs` — so informed rounds accumulating in the table cannot contaminate it. Two changes make that robust rather than lucky: (i) `M-10` (select by ledger `run_id`); (ii) `run_ab.py` writes `thread_id` + `round_index=0` + `informed_by=[]` at creation, a **record** of the designation rather than a **recovery** of it. Round-0 rows then stay a superset of the A/B cohort and can serve as an observational replication, while the claim keeps resting on the designated set. Acceptance test: with informed rounds present, `aggregate_ab.py` output is byte-identical. | A |
| **SP-3f** | P3 · *open* | **The out-of-app path is NOT closed, by design.** `SP-3d`'s button covers the in-app return leg. A user who copies a Cellwright finding into a **fresh** Hypotheses question still produces an unlinked round, and nothing in this thread stamps or blocks it. Whether that residual gap is acceptable is an **owner decision**, not something establishable from the code — recorded so it is not mistaken for an oversight. | A |

### D-PLAT · Patterns adopted from the wc-ecoli-platform critique (2026-08-03)

A six-axis critique of `wc-ecoli-platform` (read-only reference at `C:/dev/wcEcoli/interface`; **we do not modify
it and we take no code or naming from it**) returned *take one or two*. Three are recorded here as
**specifications to build** — behaviour only — and one is recorded as **rejected with its reason**. Each entry
states what theirs does, with file:line, and what ours must do **differently**; the citations are to their tree
purely as evidence for the "what theirs does" half.

| ID | P | Item | Src |
|----|---|------|-----|
| ~~**PLAT-1**~~ ✅ | **DONE 2026-08-10 — `src/cellarium/reconcile.py`, 28 tests, three verified by injection.** All five parts shipped. **(a) Envelope:** `NOT_A_MEASUREMENT` names 28 tools with the reason each is not a corpus measurement — an iML1515 FBA number, a surrogate PREDICTION for an unrun design, a proposal, a resource estimate, a web fetch, a statement about the instrument — and `mark_non_measurement` stamps the marker ON the payload in `tools.dispatch`, so it reaches the model with the number rather than as an advisory read after the sentence is written. An exhaustiveness test in the shape of `test_registry.unclassified_tools` means a tool added later must be classified or CI goes red; it cannot default into being able to ground a claim. **(b) Reconciliation:** the haystack is the CORPUS, per PLAT-R1 — a mention must resolve to a run id or design key the MANIFEST knows (382 ids / 82 designs), and then to what this conversation read. A token that is not a corpus identifier is prose, not a claim. Non-measurement calls never ground anything, so PLAT-R1's second correction (synthetic provenance fails BY CONSTRUCTION) holds structurally rather than by inspection. **(c) Annotate, never rewrite:** the original prose is always a prefix of the output, with a test and an injection. **(d) Fail closed:** `could_not_verify` is a distinct verdict; the check never reports a pass it did not earn, and returns the answer UNCHANGED if it raises. **(e)** Both `converse` exits are wired, including the forced-synthesis one — the branch where the model writes a conclusion with tools disabled and the budget spent, which is the likelier place for an ungrounded claim. ⚠️ **ONE DELIBERATE DEVIATION, and it inverts a line of this spec.** The spec keys fail-closed to the durable ledger (`CELLARIUM_EVIDENCE`). That ledger is OFF BY DEFAULT, so keying the check to it would have disabled the check by default — a guard against silent absence that is itself silently absent. The check runs off an in-memory per-conversation record (always on, a set union per call) using `evidence._harvest`, the SAME extraction the durable ledger uses; the ledger's state is reported in the payload rather than assumed. 🔍 **One defect found by RUNNING it, not by reasoning about it:** scoped strictly per turn, a correct follow-up ("as shown above, KO:argS falls") names a design read in turn 1 and gets annotated in turn 2, every time, until the reader stops reading annotations. Scope is now the CONVERSATION (`start_turn(fresh=len(messages) <= 1)`), with tests on both halves — a follow-up stays grounded, a new conversation does not inherit the last one's evidence. *(original entry follows)* **Execution envelope + post-hoc CLAIM reconciliation — with the predicate INVERTED.** *Theirs:* a non-executed action returns `"This action has NOT been executed. Do not claim it ran"` (`assistant_agent.py:736`), and after the loop `_reconcile_claim` (`:660`, called at `:1112`) scans the FINAL PROSE for claim phrases (`_CLAIM_PHRASES`, `:656`) and appends a correction when no confirmation card was surfaced. **Their predicate is UI state — "did a card get created" — and it guards unexecuted ACTIONS.** *Ours must guard unsupported CLAIMS, with a corpus predicate:* **(a) Envelope** — any tool result that did not read the corpus (a proposed design, a queued launch, a benchmark note, a literature brief) returns an explicit standing marker saying it is not a measurement, in the vocabulary D6 already uses ("a benchmark note is not a measurement"). **(b) Reconciliation** — scan Cellwright's assembled prose for *assertions about the corpus* (a channel name carrying a direction or magnitude, a design key, a run id, a viability/essentiality verdict) and cross-check each against the **run/seed/generation set actually read this turn**, which the evidence ledger already records per tool call (`evidence.py:38-45` `_ID_KEYS`/`_LABEL_KEYS`) alongside `rigor.coverage()`. **(c) On an unbacked claim, ANNOTATE, never rewrite** — append a correction naming *which* claim and *what was actually read*; silently rewriting the sentence hides the failure instead of recording it. **(d) Fail CLOSED on the unreadable case** — if the evidence ledger is off (`CELLARIUM_EVIDENCE`, `evidence.py:24`) the check reports "could not verify", never "verified"; reporting an unavailable check as a pass is the silent-absence bug class this project keeps hitting. **(e) Tests** — a planted prose claim about a design the turn never read must be caught; a correctly-cited claim must not be. | A |
| ~~**PLAT-2**~~ ✅ | **DONE 2026-08-10 — `src/cellarium/truncation.py`, 26 tests, three verified by injection. 📄 Methods record, including the two defects found while building it and the fixes: [`docs/CONTEXT_TRUNCATION.md`](docs/CONTEXT_TRUNCATION.md).** The platform's one good property is KEPT and pinned: `n_total` is captured once from the untrimmed input, so a second trim can never report "N of the already-trimmed M". **(1) Named dimensions:** the omission carries the IDENTITIES it dropped (`run_30, run_31, …`), the stratum, and which values of that stratum survived (`kept_seeds: [0,1,2,3,4]`) — in the structured `_omitted` block AND in the marker the model reads mid-payload, because a bare count says something is missing and nothing about whether it mattered. **(2) `result_schema` created:** `RESULT_SCHEMA` declares, per tool, which key holds the analysable rows, what identifies one, and which dimension a floor is counted over. 12 tools declared, 60 on a named fallback — deliberately, because a declaration is a promise about a payload's shape and writing 72 from memory would produce wrong ones; the gap is countable (`undeclared_list_tools`) rather than invisible, and a test catches a typo'd tool name, which otherwise fails silently. The omission also rides into the EVIDENCE LEDGER as its own line (`<tool>#omitted`), so a reviewer sees the same hole the agent saw instead of a complete-looking list of ids. **(3) Refusable:** trimming that leaves a replicate set below `support.MIN_SEEDS` / `MIN_GENERATIONS` returns a REFUSAL naming the scope refused and a narrower one that would qualify — the project's own constants, not a second private threshold. A refusal is returned WHOLE even when it overflows a tight cap, because slicing it produces the thing it exists to prevent. **It composes with PLAT-1:** `reconcile.forget()` removes trimmed identities from the read set, so a design the model never saw stops counting as read — without it the provenance check would report evidence the model was never shown, through its own plumbing. 🔍 **Two defects found by RUNNING it, not by reasoning:** (i) the first version fitted rows against a guessed 220-byte reserve and then appended the omission block, pushing the payload back over the cap and into the caller's last-resort string slice — the funnel emitting INVALID JSON, which is exactly what shrinking lists instead of slicing strings exists to prevent; caught at cap=900 of four probed, and the caps are now parametrized. (ii) the fix then spent the omission identities to save bytes and trimmed rows anyway, finishing with room to spare and no names at all. Fitting is now two-phase — settle the DATA first, then spend whatever is left naming what was dropped. *(original entry follows)* **Truncation with PROVENANCE OF OMISSION, and a REFUSAL FLOOR.** *Theirs:* `_compact_payload` halves the largest oversized list and stamps `"showing N of M"` (`assistant_agent.py:774-793`), correctly preserving the **original** count across repeated halvings (`:790` `original_len.setdefault`) — keep that property, a second trim must never report "N of the already-trimmed M". *Ours must go three steps further.* **(1) NAME THE DROPPED DIMENSION.** Extend the existing SP-2 informative-truncation convention (`tools.py:270-289`; `survey.py:579-593`) from *counts* to *named dimensions*: which seeds, which generations, which conditions/designs were dropped — by id, not "31 of 37". **(2) It lives in the tool's declared `result_schema` and survives into the citation trail.** There is no `result_schema` today (grep over `src/` and `tests/` returns zero hits), so this item creates it: the omission stamp is part of the tool contract rather than a note a tool may forget, and the evidence-ledger line records the omission next to the ids that *were* read, so a later reviewer sees the same omission the agent saw. **(3) Truncation is REFUSABLE.** If trimming drops the surviving set below `support.MIN_SEEDS = 2` / `MIN_GENERATIONS = 2` (`support.py:32-33`), the tool **refuses at that scope** — returning the scope it refused and a narrower scope that would qualify — rather than answering with a footnote. **The platform has no such floor:** `keep = max(1, len(lst) // 2)` (`assistant_agent.py:791`) will reduce a set to a single element and stamp "showing 1 of 34". Also required by **WELL-11b (B6)** for the data-only MCP. | A |
| **PLAT-3** | P2 | **Honest uncertainty encoding — three corrections.** *Theirs:* the interval is suppressed below n=2 and "unknown" gets a real swatch rather than being rendered as zero (`EssentialityHeatmap.tsx:18`) — both good. *Three things ours must do differently.* **(1) Put n on the FACE of the component.** Theirs hides the count in a `title` attribute (`EssentialityHeatmap.tsx:310`). Ours prints it where the value is — and prints **both** n's, seeds *and* generations, because the rule is two-dimensional by construction: seeds bound the precision, generations bound whether the quantity has settled at all (`support.py:16-18`); `support.coverage` already returns both. **(2) Suppress the MEAN too, not just the error bar.** Theirs returns a confident `mean` with `ci=None` at n=1 (`experiments.py:1500-1508`) and classifies a phenotype `"essential"` from a single completed job (`design.py:265-266`). A point estimate with no interval reads as *more* certain than one with a wide interval — the inverse of the intent. Below the floor, ours withholds the mean and renders the n and the refusal. **(3) Use STUDENT-T, not the 1.96 normal multiplier.** Theirs uses `margin = 1.96 * std / sqrt(n)` for every n ≥ 2 (`experiments.py:1510`). **MEASURED** (`scipy.stats`, this repo's `.venv`): the two-sided 0.975 multiplier is **t = 12.706 at n=2** (df=1, 6.48× the normal), **4.303 at n=3** (2.20×), **3.182 at n=4**; conversely an interval built with 1.96 delivers **70.0 % coverage at n=2** and **81.1 % at n=3** against a stated 95 %. ⚠️ **Correction to the brief that prompted this item:** it gave "at n=2, t = 4.30" — 4.303 is the **n=3** (df=2) value; at n=2 the multiplier is 12.706. The conclusion ("covers a fraction of its stated mass") holds and is now quantified. | A |
| **PLAT-R1** | — | **REJECTED, with its reason: their `check_faithfulness` grounding predicate.** `backend/eval/checks.py:177-208` defines "grounded" as **the token appears somewhere in the serialized tool output** — `haystack = json.dumps([t.get("result") for t in executed_tools])` (`:187`), then `tok.lower() in haystack_lc` (`:192`) with a 2 % rounding tolerance for numerics (`:199`). A PRNG-generated number that a tool happened to emit — a seed, an index, a job id, a synthetic placeholder — passes end-to-end, because presence in the haystack is not provenance. **Convergence risk — stated with what was actually checked.** The brief that prompted this item asserts Cellarium already runs a faithfulness check of this shape. ⚠️ **I could not locate one:** grep for `faithful` across `src/`, `evals/` and `tests/` returns only unrelated hits (`agent.py:362,384` compaction wording; `council.py:281` a temperature comment; `evals/debate.py:61,67,79` prompt text), and no substring-grounding predicate (`… in tool_output`, `json.dumps([… result …])`) exists in the tree. So treat this as a **prospective** risk to be prevented, not a defect to be found — and re-check, because an absence I searched for is weaker evidence than a presence I read. **Two corrections are binding on any such check we build:** the haystack must be the **CORPUS** (a value is grounded when it resolves to a run/seed/generation in the manifest — the same evidence set **PLAT-1** cross-checks against), not "the JSON blob of whatever ran"; and **any result carrying a synthetic or unverified-provenance marker must FAIL BY CONSTRUCTION** — surrogate output, a benchmark note, a literature value, a proposed-but-unrun design — rather than being eligible to ground a number by coincidence of substring. | A |

Audit confirmed the named sinks (Council falsifier, corpus rows, queue `from_question`) were already escaped. **Done** — see Completed. | A |

Converted the few hardcoded overlays/gradients to `--overlay`/`--zebra`/tokens; the Vega chart config now reads live tokens so interactive charts track the theme. Verified in-browser (light↔dark flip, 0 unthemed surfaces, no console errors) + smoke-test guarded. **Done** — see Completed. | A |

There is **no build system** (assets are served raw via `FileResponse`/`StaticFiles`), so a split means either a bundler (new toolchain) or load-ordered plain `<script>`s with shared globals; behavioral coverage is smoke-level only (`test_spa_smoke` checks presence, not interaction), so a subtle split-induced break wouldn't fail CI — real risk to a publication demo for marginal gain. The one cleanly-separable unit (the Vega SVG renderer, ~100 ln, one call site, sole external dep `trunc`) is the natural first extraction **if/when** a build step lands. Revisit at ~2.5k ln or when a bundler is introduced. | A |

(A full Playwright interaction test needs a browser in CI — layer on later.) | A |

Backed by `GET /api/skills` (`skills.skills_manifest()`, the SSOT — a newly vendored/authored skill appears automatically; summaries parsed from both K-Dense YAML-frontmatter and the Cellarium `# name — summary` format). Skills stay **agent-invoked** (`use_skill`); the affordances only inject a plain-language request (`"Using the <skill> skill: …"`, non-stacking). Token-themed (works light+dark); verified in-browser (chip match, palette open/close, attach, 0 console errors) + host-tested (`test_skills.py`, `test_spa_smoke.py`). **Done** — see Completed. | N |

## F · Infrastructure & hygiene

| ID | P | Item | Src |
|----|---|------|-----|
| ~~**H-1**~~ | ✅ | **CI** — GitHub Actions (`ruff` + `pytest` blocking, advisory `mypy`) on PR + push to main. Done — see Completed. | A |
| ~~**H-2**~~ | ✅ | **Tooling config** — the base `[tool.ruff]` / `[tool.mypy]` / `[tool.pytest]` config shipped with **H-1**; this tightens it by adding **import-sorting (`I`)** to the ruff select so import order is consistent + CI-enforced (24 spots auto-fixed, 1 hand-fixed). **Done** — see Completed. | A |
| ~~**H-3**~~ | ✅ | **Deps pin + run provenance** — committed **`requirements.lock`** (exact pins of the full env: core + hf/fba/rnaseq/dev extras + transitives, via `pip freeze`), and `provenance.run_environment()` (interpreter + git commit + pinned dep versions) now recorded in every Council run's meta alongside model + temperature (M-2/LLM-3) — the reproducibility bundle. **Done** — see Completed. | A |
| ~~**H-4**~~ | ✅ | **Secret scan in CI** — a `Secret scan` step (`ci.yml`, before lint) fails the build on high-signal committed secrets (Anthropic `sk-ant-` / AWS `AKIA` / GitHub `ghp_` / Slack `xox…` tokens + private keys); dependency-free `git grep`, verified green on the current tree. **Done** — see Completed. | A |
| ~~**H-7**~~ | ✅ | **In-app credential vault + Settings panel** — the CLI-shaped wall in front of Tier 1 is gone: a gear-icon drawer takes the Anthropic key and stores it in the **OS keychain** (`credentials.py`, optional `keyring` extra), injected into `os.environ` once at boot so all twelve `anthropic.Anthropic()` sites are untouched. Precedence: exported var > repo-root `.env` > keychain — which also **fixed a real latent bug**: `apps/server.py` never called `load_dotenv`, so the README's `cp .env.example .env` had never worked for the web app. Four invariants pinned by `tests/test_credentials.py` (35 cases): the key never enters the LLM context (no tool exists — same containment as `/api/approve`), never crosses HTTP (masked-only `sk-ant-…AB12`), is never written to disk in plaintext (**no secure backend ⇒ session-only, never a silent downgrade**), and every outbound message is redacted. Verified end-to-end against the real Windows Credential Manager (store → restart → clear) and in-browser. | R |
| ~~**H-8**~~ | ✅ | **Credential scrub at the three funnels** (`redact.py`) — from a 6-agent leak-surface audit (`wf_893d1bba`) which **empirically reproduced** a leak in pre-existing code: a key with a trailing newline (the classic paste artifact, and the Settings field is now a paste target) makes httpx raise `Illegal header value b'sk-ant-…\n'`; the outer SDK exception is clean but `traceback.format_exc()` is not, and `tools.py` logs internal defects with `exc_info=True`. Fixed at the funnels everything drains through: `server._jsonsafe` (→ browser → localStorage), `agent._truncate_tool_result` (→ model context → SQLite → past compaction), a `logging.Filter` (the only hook that reaches rendered `exc_info`), and `hypotheses.fail` (durable SQLite error row). `redact` holds no secret and reads no env, so `agent.py` can import it without breaching the "agent cannot reach the vault" boundary. Also closed a **CI blind spot**: the secret scan is `git grep -I`, which skips binaries, so the tracked `data/sessions.seed.db` was never scanned — now byte-scanned in `test_redact.py`. | R |
| ~~**H-9**~~ | ✅ | **Leak-surface follow-ups closed** (`wf_893d1bba`) — none were exploitable (the key is not in the agent's context, so it could not be exfiltrated); these are defence for if that ever changes. (a) **`web_get`**: the host allow-list already existed, but `urlopen` **follows 30x to any host**, so an allow-listed host redirecting elsewhere was a straight bypass — now every hop is re-checked (`_AllowListRedirects`); plus an allow-list of header NAMES (no Authorization/Cookie/X-*), an http(s)-only scheme check, and a refusal if the URL or any header value is credential-shaped. The vendored `paper-lookup` skill already documents "Fetch tools can't set headers", so no regression; verified live against OpenAlex / NCBI eutils / UniProt (all 200). (b) **`redact.child_env()`** on all five `subprocess.run` sites (`runner`×2, `reader`, `resources`, `provenance`) — copy-and-remove, never build-from-scratch, so Windows keeps SYSTEMROOT and every OS keeps PATH; a lint test fails if a new `subprocess.run` appears without it. Docker was never the exposure (`docker run` passes only explicit `-e`); this closes the host-side CLI child. (c) scrub in **`SessionStore.put`** (the most durable sink — the model's full context, replayed every turn) and in **`launch.py`**'s persisted `str(exc)`. (d) **`record_demo_sessions.py --seed` refuses** to write the tracked seed DB if a transcript carries key-shaped text — refuse, not scrub, because that would be a leak to investigate. | R |
| ~~**H-10**~~ | ✅ | **Adversarial review of the credential work** (`wf_119eb7a2` — 5 attack agents × independent verifiers). **Two confirmed defects, both mine, both fixed:** (i) `_local_only`'s Origin check was a string **prefix** match, so `http://localhost.evil.example` satisfied `startswith("http://localhost")` — an attacker only had to *register a name beginning with a loopback literal*, no DNS rebinding needed. Found independently by four agents and reproduced against the running app; now a parsed-hostname comparison. (ii) `clear()` gated the keychain delete on the *current* secure verdict, so a key stored when a keychain was available survived a later run where it wasn't — while the UI reported it removed. Now always attempts the delete. **Kept as cheap defence-in-depth** though verifiers rated them unreachable: the `scrub_obj` depth-guard/dict-KEY escapes (closed by scrubbing the serialised form), `HypothesisStore._write` (scrub at the chokepoint, not per-call-site), the `SessionStore` in-memory/on-disk inconsistency, and `redact.register_secret()` for **shape-less** keys (a gateway/Bedrock token matches no known prefix, so patterns alone can't see it — the vault pushes the literal in; `redact` still reads no env and no keychain, so the module boundary holds). **Confirmed held under attack:** I1 (no tool in the 57-name dispatch can read a file, run a shell, or dump the environment; `credentials` is not imported by `agent.py`); DNS rebinding (the Host check); cross-origin *reads* of `/api/settings` (empty middleware stack, no CORS); LAN exposure (every launch path pins 127.0.0.1); the `web_get` egress guards under a sweep of uppercase/trailing-dot/port/userinfo/`%40`/`%2F`/IDN-homograph/protocol-relative/CRLF variants; and that the redirect re-check *is* actually installed by `build_opener`. | R |
| ~~**H-11**~~ | ✅ | **Cross-OS behaviour, settled and CI-enforced.** Persistence to a real keychain works on **Windows** (`WinVaultKeyring`, verified live here end-to-end), **macOS** (`macOS.Keyring`), and a **Linux desktop** with an unlocked GNOME/KDE keyring. Everywhere else — headless Linux, SSH, systemd, Docker, **WSL2**, BSD — keyring resolves to `backends.fail` and we **degrade, not break**: the button becomes "Use for this session", a plain-language note says why, and `.env`/exported vars (which take precedence anyway) remain the documented path. A parametrised test pins the verdict for each platform's real backend class. Three fixes came out of the portability review: (i) **WSL2 boot hang** — `import keyring` can hang when `DISPLAY` is set with no X server (D-Bus autolaunch timeout, jaraco/keyring#531); it ran at server boot, and a hang is not an exception, so `try/except` would not have saved it — the probe now runs in a thread with an 8 s join; (ii) `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` (+`AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID`) added to `child_env`'s strip list — the SDK's gateway/proxy bearer variable, exactly the shape-less class patterns can't see; (iii) a blocked redirect reported a bare `HTTP 302` because `web_get`'s own handler swallowed the refusal message. ⏳ Known and documented, not bugs: macOS re-prompts when the Python executable changes (per-executable keychain ACL); a macOS SSH session with a locked keychain reads as "no key set"; and any non-loopback deployment (container by bridge IP, `--host 0.0.0.0`) correctly 403s the credential endpoints. | R |
| ~~**H-12**~~ | ✅ | **Per-page CSRF token on the credential write path.** The review reproduced an attacker-origin POST **writing a key into the real Windows Credential Manager**. The parsed-Origin fix (H-10) closes the demonstrated path, but `Sec-Fetch-Site` still *fails open* when absent (Safari < 16.4, embedded WebViews) and a legacy form POST can omit `Origin` too — so browser-vintage was load-bearing. `index.html` now carries a per-process token (`__CSRF__`, substituted at serve time) that every **mutating** credential call must echo in `X-Cellarium-CSRF`; a cross-origin page cannot read the document, so it cannot forge one. Reads stay open. Verified live: SPA save succeeds, the same POST without the header returns 403. | R |
| **H-13** | P2 | **Credential tests: what is still unverified, and what needs re-running.** Spec: [`docs/CREDENTIALS.md`](docs/CREDENTIALS.md). **(a) Unverified platforms — simulation only.** `test_backend_verdict_per_platform` asserts the verdict for each platform's *backend class*, but only **Windows** has had a real end-to-end round-trip (store → restart → clear, on WinVaultKeyring). **macOS Keychain** and **Linux GNOME/KDE** are classifier-simulated; they need one human run each on real hardware — including the macOS **re-prompt on a changed Python executable** and the **KDE `python3-dbus`** requirement, neither of which any test covers. **(b) The WSL2 8-second boot-timeout path has never fired for real** — it is written against jaraco/keyring#531, not against a reproduced hang; worth one run on a WSL2 box with `DISPLAY` set and no X server. **(c) Recurring — the live `web_get` check.** `_ALLOWED_HOSTS` is only meaningful if those hosts still answer; the OpenAlex / NCBI-eutils / UniProt fetches were run by hand, are network-dependent, and are deliberately **not** in CI. Re-run them when a literature skill misbehaves or a host is added — a host that starts 30x-ing off the allow-list now fails closed, which is correct but looks like an outage. **(d) Recurring — re-run the adversarial review** (`wf_119eb7a2`) whenever the credential surface changes or Starlette/keyring majors bump; the two real defects it found (prefix-matched Origin, gated `clear()`) were both in code that already had passing tests, so the suite alone is not sufficient evidence here. ~~**(e)** No test covers the **CSRF token rotating on server restart**.~~ **✅ CLOSED** — `tests/test_credentials.py:333-359` exercises the whole flow: all three mutating endpoints return 403 on the stale token, reads stay open, and a fresh client re-reading the shell gets the NEW token and succeeds. (Already recorded in `WELL-6z7` item 8; this row was simply never updated.) **(a)-(d) remain open.** | R |
| **H-14** | P2 | **Replace the regex secret scan with a file-type-aware scanner (gitleaks).** **H-4**'s `git grep` was silently red-in-perpetuity: `AKIA[0-9A-Z]{16}` matched **40 lines** of `vendor/v301/reconstruction/ecoli/flat/proteins.tsv`, where amino-acid single-letter codes are exactly `[A-Z]` — `git grep -hoIE 'AKIA[0-9A-Z]{16}' -- vendor` returns protein 20-mers, not keys. Because the step `exit 1`s on any match, **`ruff` and `pytest` never ran on any PR**; a green-looking H-4 was actually a permanently-failing gate. **Fixed now by scoping** (`ci.yml:23-51`) — `':(exclude)vendor/'` + `':(exclude)model_overlay/'`, both upstream wcEcoli source, the latter also sha256-pinned in `model_overlay/MANIFEST.json` so the overlay guard catches any change (`docs/OVERLAY.md:83,113-114`). **The scoping is the patch, not the cure.** A regex over TSV **biological** data is the wrong instrument: the corpus is full of long uppercase strings (protein sequences, gene/EC identifiers, `MONOMER`/`RXN` ids) whose alphabet is a superset of every base32/base36 token alphabet, so *any* prefix-plus-uppercase-run pattern will keep colliding, and each collision is resolved by widening an exclusion — i.e. the scanner monotonically loses coverage over exactly the directories that grow. **gitleaks** is the right replacement: it scores **Shannon entropy** on the candidate (a real key is high-entropy; the 20-mer `AKIA`+`AILEEPPRMGNSDLGV` is drawn from a 20-letter residue alphabet with protein-like statistics, so it does **not** clear the bar) and it is **file-type/path aware** (`[allowlist]` by path regex, per-rule, versioned in `.gitleaks.toml` — reviewable, unlike an ever-growing pathspec list in YAML). It also scans **history**, not just the working tree, which the current step cannot do at all. Ship it as an added step first (non-blocking, compare findings against the regex step for one cycle), then make it the blocking one and delete the `git grep`. **Note the binary blind spot too** — `git grep -I` skips binaries; H-8 patched that for `data/sessions.seed.db` by byte-scanning in `test_redact.py`, which is a per-file workaround gitleaks would subsume. **Whatever replaces it must keep a negative control**: this fix was verified by planting AWS's documented example key (`AKIA`+`IOSFODNN7EXAMPLE`) in `data/` (scan → exit **1**, reported) and in `vendor/` (**not** reported, so the exclusion is scoping and not pattern-weakening), then removing both (exit **0**). A scan that cannot fail is not a scan, and H-4 shipped without that control. **Side-finding:** the literals above are written **split** because an intact one in this file (or in `ci.yml`) trips the scanner — the current design cannot document its own false positives, which is itself an argument for a tool with a real allowlist. | N |
| **H-15** | P1 | **Local absolute paths are published in the HuggingFace manifest artifact.** MEASURED in `data/manifest/vmnik-compact.parquet` (322 rows): the **`note`** column carries the host filesystem layout in **14 rows** — 12 with `C:\\dev\\anthropic_hackathon\\runs` (JSON-escaped) and 2 with the unescaped form — plus **12 rows** containing `/wcEcoli/out`. They are crash notes that captured a full `docker run` argv, e.g. `sim crashed: Command '['docker', 'run', '--rm', '-v', 'C:\\dev\\anthropic_hackathon\\runs:/wcEcoli/out', …]`. `simout_path` is clean (**0** rows with `C:\dev`), so this is confined to `note`. Not a credential, but it discloses a contributor's username and directory tree in a **published** artifact, and it is the reason `data/` is **deliberately not excluded** from the secret scan (H-14) — blinding the scanner to the only directory with a known disclosure would be the wrong trade. Fix at the write path (scrub the argv before it reaches `note`, as `redact.py` does at the three funnels in H-8), backfill the 14 rows, and add a manifest-publication test that fails on any host-path-shaped string in a free-text column. Decide separately whether re-publishing needs a superseding revision on HF. | N |
| ~~**H-16**~~ | ✅ | **ROUTE1 extension simulations: recorded, then DELETED.** The gate held — [`docs/ROUTE1_CORPUS_RECORD.md`](docs/ROUTE1_CORPUS_RECORD.md) was written, verified and committed (`65137bf`) **before** anything was removed, and the deletion re-derived its own target list from each run's `metadata.json` rather than reading the doc back. **DELETED 127 of 128 candidates: 72.385 GB, 43 261 files, 0 failures.** `C:/dev/wcEcoli/out` went 136 entries / 80.700 GB / 49 407 files → **9 entries / 8.315 GB / 6 146 files** (independent confirmation of the same total). Disk free 79.172 → 138.099 GB; the **13.458 GB shortfall against 72.385 is UNKNOWN, not attributed** — 0 hardlinks, 0 compressed/sparse files in a 200-file sample, and `vssadmin` exits 2 (not elevated) so shadow-copy retention could not be ruled out. **Preserved first:** the 17 loose analysis scripts are in [`archive/route1_analysis_scripts/`](archive/route1_analysis_scripts/) (commit `a32c590`), all md5-verified byte-identical **before** their originals went — they *define* the interventions the record cites (`_aa_kcat_throttle.py`, `_ks_throttle.py`, `_r1s_extract.py`); and `_r1s_npz` (25 MB) was **retained in place**, the only surviving per-timestep Φ/`D`/`kmax` record of the `mf` ladder. **Manifest impact: NONE, re-verified AFTER deletion** — 0 of 322 raw rows resolve under `C:/dev/wcEcoli/out` and 0 name any deleted entry, so `manifest.drop_run` was correctly **not** invoked and no tombstone was written. **Survivors verified by name:** `kinetic_parca`, `operonsON_kin_probe`, `operons_off_parca` (protected) plus the five still **NEEDS-DECISION** (`refit_none`/`refit2_none`/`refit_A055w3`/`refit_shipped`/`kinetic_parca_operons_off`, 5.665 GB — untouched, still undecided). ⚠️ One irreversible consequence recorded in §7: the `km_parca` K_M-modified kb (`afb48d8c…`) existed only in ROUTE1 directories and is gone — regenerating it needs the extension repo's ParCa. Also **removed the `extension` git remote** from `.git/config`. | N |

Full thread, the 16-invariant catalogue, the two enforcement gaps and the placement proposal: [**F-HYG** below](#f-hyg--corpus-hygiene--the-thread); build items `H-17a…H-17e`. | A |
