### F-HYG · Corpus hygiene — the thread

*Opened 2026-08-03. **This is its own thread because it is broader than any one surface.** The rules that make a
corpus number *mean* something live today in three places a stranger never opens — `support.py`, `capability.py`,
and tribal knowledge — and anyone who clones Cellarium can reach the same Parquet by **CLI, web app, a future MCP
surface, or `duckdb` at a shell prompt**. Only the Python route touches any of them. Every invariant below was
**learned expensively**, most of them from a defect this project actually shipped; the catalogue is the working
source, and `H-17a` is the item that moves it to where a stranger will actually meet it (a machine-readable
`INVARIANTS.json` + the dataset card), because a rule that lives only in a task list is not a contract.*

**State of the corpus, MEASURED 2026-08-03** (`data/manifest/*.parquet` via `.venv`, `manifest.DEDUP_QUALIFY`
applied; re-verified independently for this record):

- **322 raw rows → 322 after dedup** (the dedup rule is currently a **no-op** on the compacted shard) and **0
  tombstones**. Dedup and tombstones are therefore *dev-corpus hygiene*, **not** the load-bearing invariants —
  the ones below are semantic and survive a clean corpus of unique runs.
- **101 of 322 rows are `reportable=False`** — `crashed` 63 · `no_division` 14 · `implausible_channel` 11 ·
  `noop_knockout` 7 · `over_replicated` 4 · `empty` 2. They sit in the table and are **shaped exactly like data**.
- Of the **44** design keys with a reportable row, only **21 (48 %)** clear both `support.MIN_SEEDS = 2` and
  `MIN_GENERATIONS = 2` (`support.py:32-33`); **8** are single-seed and **23** have max depth < 2. A stranger
  hits the evidential floor immediately, not eventually.
- **7 modules issue their own `read_parquet`** (`audit.py`, `manifest.py`, `miase.py`, `operons.py`,
  `similarity.py`, `store.py`, `survey.py`) — a direct reader is the 8th. **There is no single read boundary.**

#### The catalogue — 16 invariants ESTABLISHED from the code

Each row: what it is · what a caller does wrong without it · the evidence and the incident that taught it ·
evidential standing. (1)–(3) are the three the owner named; (4)–(16) are the ones established from the code in
addition to them.

| # | Invariant | What goes wrong without it | Evidence + the incident | Standing |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **INDEPENDENCE** — seeds are independent; **generations within a lineage are not** | counting the nine (seed, generation) cells of a 3×3 design as **n = 9** rather than n = 3 | `support.py:16-17,32-33`; the case is written up in `docs/ROUTE1_CORPUS_RECORD.md:136-140` — the inflated n made a paired test reach significance **on an arm that was mathematically incapable of having an effect**. `stats.py:46-57` holds the cluster machinery *and* the record of two **withdrawn** cluster variables (machine: ICC 0.0 at fixed depth; depth: a **fixed**, not random, effect) | MEASURED (from the record) |
| **2** | **COMPARABILITY** — elongation model, operon setting, medium **and `kb_sha256`** partition the corpus | pooling runs from two partitions into one mean | `capability.py:29-40` — the 86-wide `fraction_trna_charged` is a broadcast identity / 86 independent values / 86 exact zeros **depending on mode**; `manifest.py:275-294` (`_design_tag` puts the mode **into identity**); `factors.py:248-255` (`one_factor_neighbours` refuses a cross-mode control: same elongation model is "a precondition, not a fourth factor"); `audit.py:58-63`. `capability.py` refuses on the elongation axis; **nothing refuses on `kb_sha256`** → `M-11`. **MEASURED 2026-08-03 — `kb_sha256` IS UNSOUND IN ONE DIRECTION and `M-11` must not be built on it as written.** Two ParCa runs of the SAME image, same inputs, same `--cpus 14`, minutes apart, produce **different hashes** (`94325a1e…` vs `9881c39e…`) whose `exp_ppgpp` is **bit-identical (0/3276)** and whose simulations are **bitwise identical over all 2530 timesteps** (ppGpp and cellMass). So ParCa's BEHAVIOUR is deterministic and only its SERIALISATION is not: `same hash ⇒ same kb` holds, **`different hash ⇒ different experiment` does NOT** — it reports FALSE partitions, refusing legitimate pooling and inflating the count of distinct baselines. **FIXED**: `reader.kb_content_hash` (new `kb_content_hash` mode in `_reader_worker.py`) walks the unpickled sim_data object graph — ordering by attribute/key name, hashing numpy dtype+shape+bytes — rather than naming fields, because any hand-picked field list is a guess that silently misses what it omits. VERIFIED on real data: the two fits with different FILE hashes now hash **identically** (`99ab9368…`), a genuinely different kb (native + phnE1 reverted) stays **distinct** (`624d5a9f…`), and coverage is 415,137 nodes / 17,714 arrays / 20 skipped / 0 depth-capped. `provenance.same_kb(a,b)` is the predicate: it returns **None (undecidable), never False**, when all it has is two differing file hashes. `kb_provenance()` now reports `kb_content_sha256`. **KNOWN LIMIT, measured and documented in-code:** it answers *same knowledge base*, NOT *same output* — the fork kb (`c1bd1018…`) and the phnE1-reverted kb (`624d5a9f…`) hash differently yet give **bitwise identical** simulations for `gltX+relA+spoT`, differing only in fields that design never reads. Remaining: add the column to the manifest schema and route the comparability checks through `same_kb`. | CODE-READ + MEASURED |
| **3** | **REPORTABILITY** — crash rows and QC-failed runs are in the table and look like data | `WHERE reportable` deletes the **lethality phenotype**; *not* filtering silently Welch-tests crashed runs | `qc.py:62-65` — reportable iff **every** generation is ok; `survey.py:197-198` drops non-reportable and tombstoned rows; `differential.py:28` records the version that did **not** filter. MEASURED: 101/322 non-reportable (breakdown above) — and some of them are **results, not failures** | MEASURED |
| **4** | **NULL IS NOT FALSE** — unmeasured must never read as a negative observation | SQL three-valued logic and Python truthiness return **two different corpora, with no error on either** | `store.py:107-112` — 36 of 272 rows had NULL viability columns and `bool(None)` turned *not measured* into *did not divide*, producing **three false IMPAIRED verdicts**. MEASURED today: **26 rows have `crashed IS NULL` and all 26 are `reportable=True`**; `WHERE NOT crashed` returns **233 of 322** (the 26 are dropped), while Python `if not r['crashed']` keeps them | MEASURED |
| **5** | **GENERATION DEPTH IS A STRATUM, NOT NOISE** | comparing designs at unequal depth, believing you are averaging over generations | A channel is the **last** generation's time-mean (`_reader_worker.py:203`, `_dynamics(gs[-1])`); recorded measurement: `growth_rate == per_generation[-1]` on **91/91** rows, `== mean over generations` on **0/91**. So depth *selects which generation you read*: ICC **0.6795** at the reported index vs **0.0000** at matched generation 0; **six designs flip sign**; `rRNA_KO:2op` −7.4 % → +2.2 % (`survey.py:44-90`). `support.compare():93-110` is the depth check. MEASURED today, all 322 rows: depth 0:65 · 1:125 · 3:13 · 4:108 · 7:7 · 8:4 — **unequal depth is the norm** | MEASURED |
| **6** | **IDENTITY IS THE `ko_index` SET, NEVER THE GENE NAME** | treating an alias as a distinct experiment, or a design as a single-gene knockout | `factors.py:11-20` — 4,538 genes collapse onto **2,984** indices; **1,554 names are aliases**; `KO:pheS`/`pheT`/`pheM`/`thrS`/`infC`/`ihfA` are **one** run. `KO:flgB` deletes nine genes; `KO:rpoB` does **not** silence rpoB; `KO:rpmJ` silences `secY` (operons-ON semantics, `docs/KNOCKOUT_SEMANTICS.md`). `factors.dedupe():262-273`, invariant D4 (`manifest.py:810`). MEASURED: **7 rows** carry `qc='noop_knockout'` — a wild type wearing a KO label | MEASURED |
| **7** | **NO DESIGN KEY MAY BE BUILT FROM A NULLABLE COLUMN** | two **opposite** experiments merging into one design and averaging | `survey.py:95-118` — every `timeline` run stores `condition=NULL`, so an amino-acid **upshift** and a **downshift** both key to `timeline/None` and report as *4 seeds of one design*. Invariant D3 (`manifest.py:809`), CI-pinned in `tests/test_corpus_integrity.py:52-73` | CODE-READ |
| **8** | **RECORDER TRUNCATION** — a fixed-width string column silently loses data | trusting the recorded timeline as what actually ran | `serialization.py:1-30` — `FBAResults/media_id` is sized from its **first** value, so `'minimal_plus_amino_acids'[:7] == 'minimal'` and a nutrient shift **vanishes from the record while the sim performed it**. Numeric damage (`segments.py:1-30`): one segment spanning the whole generation reported `fba_objective` ≈ 7.9 for a quantity going 0.81 → 14.1. Detector is cross-run dtype-width comparison — **the corpus convicts itself**, no external expectation needed | CODE-READ + MEASURED |
| **9** | **IN-SAMPLE ≠ OUT-OF-SAMPLE** | reading agreement in a ParCa-**fitted** condition as predictive validation | `provenance.py:1-27` — the fitted set is deliberately conservative and pinned by `test_provenance.test_in_sample_set_is_pinned`. **Hygiene gap:** the tag is computed at *read* time in Python (`store.py:56-57`) and is **not a column**, so a non-Python reader never sees it | CODE-READ |
| **10** | **A MISSING MECHANISM IS A REFUSAL, NEVER A ZERO** | reporting an algebraic identity as a measurement | `capability.py:1-15` — a within-family tRNA charging spread of `0.00e+00` was an identity at `polypeptide_elongation.py:163`, reported as a finding. The `check()` contract (`capability.py:536-539`): a False `can_answer` **always** carries a refusal and **never** a value | CODE-READ |
| **11** | **APPEND-ONLY, SUPERSEDE, NEVER MUTATE — AND FILTER *AFTER* DEDUP** | a stale row surviving a valid correction | `manifest.py:66-72` — a correction that rewrote `simout_path` rewrote **half the dedup key** and minted a new row instead of superseding. `manifest.py:867-871` — SQL applies `WHERE` **before** `QUALIFY`, so filtering first dedups *among the offending rows alone* and reports the stale row **even after a valid correction**: an invariant unsatisfiable by correction. Tombstones (`manifest.drop_run():100-150`, `survey._mark_dropped():165-175`) are excluded from ranking but **kept in coverage**; a drop with no reason is refused | CODE-READ |
| **12** | **ORPHANS AND PHANTOMS** — readable on disk ≠ visible to any query, and vice versa | an entire design silently absent from an audit | invariant D7 (`manifest.py:813`): a whole `valS` design stayed invisible through an entire audit because it was **readable but unindexed** (`tests/test_corpus_integrity.py:256-258`). The reverse — indexed, run dir gone — is still valid for channels (`manifest.reconcile_disk():928+`) | CODE-READ |
| **13** | **RAW-LOCAL ≠ INDEXED ≠ DOWNLOADABLE; unverified is never "absent"** | refusing a legitimate pull, or reporting a public dataset as missing | `hf.py:28-42` — a remnant run dir counted as *local*, so `download_raw` refused a legitimate pull while gene-level tools failed. `hf.py:189-211` — HF presence is `True`/`False`/**`None`**, and a download command is emitted **only when presence is CONFIRMED**. The recorded incident (`hf.py:78-85`): a stale OAuth token made `HfApi()` 401 on a **public** dataset while `HfApi(token=False)` succeeded at the same moment, every row reported "available: false", and the wrong diagnosis was filed. This is the **silent-absence bug class** at the storage boundary | CODE-READ |
| **14** | **ENVELOPE** — some runs are data-shaped but not interpretable | quoting channels from mechanistically invalid generations | `envelope.py:1-8,30` — a mid-simulation carbon switch to acetate/succinate/fumarate/malate desynchronises replication–division; those carbon sources exist only as **static fitted conditions** | CODE-READ |
| **15** | **AGGREGATE COLUMNS HIDE THE SHAPE; not every column is an observable** | concluding "charging is fine" from a mean that cannot show the phenotype | the manifest's `fraction_trna_charged` is a **single mean over families** and cannot show cognate-family de-charging — one family starves while ~19 stay loaded (`tools.py:1241`). `survey.py:25` excludes `fba_objective` from the biological ranking as a **solver diagnostic** | CODE-READ |
| **16** | **THE KNOWLEDGE BASE IS PART OF THE EXPERIMENT** — operon mode is a **ParCa** option, not a sim option | treating two ParCa builds as rows in one comparison | there is **no per-simulation override**, so switching produces a different `simData.cPickle`: the two modes are separate **arms**, never rows in one comparison (`operons.py` MECHANISM). Every row carries `operons` + `kb_sha256` (`manifest.py:379`, `provenance.kb_provenance():71-120`). Note the operon half is **ARGUED from the ParCa mechanism, not measured** — all 322 rows are `operons='on'` and no knockout has ever run under OFF | CODE-READ / ARGUED |
| **17** | **A GENERATION CAN BE MISSING ITS END AND STILL READ AS COMPLETE** — the last timestep of generation *n* must meet the first timestep of generation *n+1* | quoting a generation's duration, final mass, or time-mean from a lineage whose data stops before the division that ended it | MEASURED 2026-08-03, item 7. Auditing **all 269 generation boundaries** in the local corpus (`Main/time` of gen *n* vs gen *n+1*): **2 have a gap** — `wildtype_374656/000000` gen 0 ends at **2047 s** while gen 1 starts at **2530 s** (**482 timesteps, 19.1 %, missing**), and `wildtype_374656/000001` gen 0 ends at **478 s** while gen 1 starts at **2574 s** (**2095 s, 81.4 %, missing**). **Both lack `Daughter*_inherited_state.cPickle`; every intact generation has it.** The manifest reports the seed-0 lineage as `qc='ok'`, `generation_qc` **all `"ok"`**, `reportable=True` — so **`generation_qc` does not detect end-truncation**. Damage measured: the truncated span made the fork-era wildtype look **+23.50 %** shorter-lived than its native rerun, when the true division times are **2529 s vs 2529 s (+0.00 %)**; and a chaos-floor CV computed from recorded spans read **13.29 %** against **8.85 %** from start-to-start cycle times (n=24). **Detector:** start-to-start deltas, which are immune to end-truncation; **daughter-state presence** is the second, independent signal. **Cause UNESTABLISHED** — the directory-collision hypothesis (`runner._variant_index` omits `generations` from its key) was **REFUTED**: the two rows per seed sit under **different output roots** (`runs/aadrop` vs `runs/cellarium`) with **different `kb_sha256`**, so they never shared a path. **FIXED (detector, not cause):** `_reader_worker` now records `t_start`/`t_end` per generation (both construction sites), `GenerationResult` carries them, and `qc.truncated_generations` / `qc.check_result` compare generation *n*'s end against *n+1*'s start — a gap > `MAX_GENERATION_GAP_SEC` (5 s; healthy boundaries measure ~1 s) marks it `QCStatus.TRUNCATED`, which makes the lineage non-reportable. Generations with no recorded times are **skipped, not assumed continuous**; the terminal generation is never flagged (no witness); a worse status (`dead`, `no_division`) is never overwritten; a hole in the lineage is not guessed across. Both real cases are pinned as tests, with a non-vacuity test on the intact boundaries of the same design. **The CAUSE remains unestablished.** | MEASURED |

**Two gaps, both MEASURED, both open:**

- **LATENT — the design key cannot separate two knowledge bases.** `survey.design_key()` (`survey.py:124-126`)
  is `perturbation/tag`; the tag carries `#elong:` but **not** `operons` or `kb_sha256`, and `_deduped_rows`
  (`survey.py:129-162`) does not even **select** those columns — so `analysis_rows()` (`survey.py:178-205`),
  *the* row source for every comparison tool, is **structurally unable** to keep two knowledge bases apart.
  Independently re-verified: the corpus carries two `kb_sha256` values (279 / 43 rows) and **2 of 44 reportable
  design keys mix them**, including `wildtype/basal` — `differential.REFERENCE` (`differential.py:20`), the
  reference for **every** comparison. → **`M-11`**.
- **ENFORCEMENT — there is no single read boundary.** 7 modules issue their own `read_parquet` and a cloner at
  a `duckdb` prompt is the 8th. `store.list_results()` applies `DEDUP_QUALIFY` but **not** `_mark_dropped`, and
  does not filter `reportable` (`store.py:49-58`) — while feeding `hf._design_seeds`,
  `manifest.integrity_check` **and the UI**. Drift of exactly this shape has already happened: `rigor.disconfirm`
  — the tool whose job is to **challenge** a claim — reported an interval **5.5× narrower** than `survey_corpus`,
  over crashed runs, under a colliding key (`survey.py:178-189`). A disconfirmation tool more confident than the
  thing it checks is worse than no tool.

#### Where they must live — three options, three different populations

**None of them alone is sufficient**, and the reason is the population each one reaches: a **direct Parquet
reader** is reachable only by (B) and the dataset card; the **Python API user** by (A); an **agent or MCP client**
by (C).

| Option | What it is | Buys | Costs / limits |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A** | **One enforcing module / one read boundary** — `hygiene.rows(purpose=…) -> (rows, ctx)`, `ctx` naming every filter applied and every refusal raised; purposes `analysis` / `coverage` / `audit` | ends read-path drift, which has **already happened** (the `disconfirm` 5.5× interval; `store.list_results` above) | **≥3 purposes are mandatory, not optional** — `audit._rows()` needs **un-deduped** rows to see supersession (`audit.py:44`) and `survey.lethality()` needs the **non-reportable** rows because collapse *is* the phenotype (`survey.py:244-250`). ~7 call-site migrations + tests (an undercount — it counts `read_parquet` sites, not the consumers downstream of `store.list_results`/`analysis_rows`). **Unenforceable against an outsider**: a cloner at a `duckdb` prompt bypasses Python entirely. Buys internal consistency, not external safety |
| **B1** | Hive-partition the published corpus by `elongation_model=/operons=/kb_sha256=` | pooling across a partition requires **naming two directories** — the category error becomes visible in the query text | rigid (a second wcEcoli fork forces a re-layout), and it **cannot express independence at all**, which is about how rows are *counted*, not where they live |
| **B2** | **Non-nullable, non-defaultable columns** — no NULL `crashed`, no NULL `condition` (write `shift_direction` explicitly) | kills the 3VL trap (26 rows) and the `timeline/None` merge **at the source** | write-time only: protects the published dataset, not a contributor's un-reindexed local shard |
| **B3** | **Publish the unit of analysis** — `lineage_id` + `generation_index` per row | a caller who groups by `lineage_id` **cannot** treat 9 lineage-generation cells as n = 9 — independence becomes structurally unskippable | changes **what a row is** (today: a run whose channels are the last generation's time-mean). A corpus **rebuild**, not a metadata edit → schedule **with** the production build. Compatibility with the existing `per_generation` / `generation_qc` JSON columns is **unexamined** |
| **B4** | **Ship the refusal-carrying fields as real columns** — `reportable`/`qc`/`crashed` already are; `provenance` is read-time only; capability refusals are Python-only | nearly free, and reaches **every reader in every language** | does not carry the *counting* rules |
| **C** | **Refusal at the read boundary, scoped to the CLAIM not the access** — every read returns its own support block and refuses the specific claim it cannot support | the shape already exists: `support.attach()` (`support.py:76-90`), `capability.check()` (`capability.py:536`) | binds only callers who come through the boundary, and the refusal **must** be scoped (refuse *quote a mean*, allow *show me the rows*). **The M-7 advisory-gate precedent is the standing warning**: a blocking version parked 23 of 25 canonical questions — a hygiene gate that blocks is a worse failure than the one it prevents |

**RECOMMENDATION (INFERRED, from the population each option reaches):** sequence **B4 + B2** → **A** → **C
layered on A's `ctx`** → **B3 with the production corpus build**, plus a machine-readable **`INVARIANTS.json`**
loaded by the module, the dataset card **and** the MCP surface, and **probed** by `manifest.integrity_check`
(new codes: **D10** NULL in a filter column · **D11** design key omits a partitioning key present in the corpus ·
**D12** row missing its `generations` stratum). The probe is not optional: `capability.py` already proved the
pattern — it declares markers and `probe()` greps the checkout so a stale declaration is caught
(`capability.py:19-22,595-633`). **A declaration nobody verifies is a comment, and this codebase has been burned
by comments that were true when written.**

| ID | P | Item | Src |
|----|---|------|-----|
| ~~**H-17a**~~ ✅ | **DONE 2026-08-10 — `data/INVARIANTS.json` + `src/cellarium/invariants.py`, 20 tests, three verified by injection.** All four parts shipped. **The catalogue is published machine-readably** — 17 invariants GENERATED FROM the F-HYG table rather than retyped, so fidelity is by construction — with each entry naming the `integrity_check` code that VERIFIES it **or naming null WITH the reason there is none**. `invariants.coverage()` reports **8 of 17 verified** and why the other 9 are not: "17 invariants" and "8 enforced invariants" are different claims about a corpus and only one of them is true. A test greps `integrity_check`'s source for every code the catalogue names, so a claimed probe that does not exist goes red — the `capability.probe()` pattern, applied to the catalogue itself. **B4:** `provenance` now ships as a REAL COLUMN (it was read-time-only, i.e. Python-only, so a cloner at a duckdb prompt got no in-sample flag at all and could read agreement in a ParCa-fitted condition as validation). **B2:** `crashed` is coerced to a real bool and `condition` is never NULL — a timeline run now writes the SHIFT DIRECTION explicitly (`shift:up:minimal->minimal_plus_amino_acids` vs `shift:down:…`), which is exactly the pair INV-7 records as having merged into `timeline/None` and averaged as four seeds of one design. An unparseable timeline writes `shift:unparsed` — a statement about the RECORD, never a guessed direction. **D10/D11/D12 added**, and `backfill_condition` filled the **16** historical NULLs D10 found (0 refused; it refuses any row where writing the column would MOVE `design_tag`, because fixing a NULL by re-identifying an experiment is the worse defect). Corpus: **418 rows before and after, `integrity_check` ok=True, n_drift=0**. ⚠️ **A new distinction that keeps the probes switched on: DRIFT vs STANDING.** D11 fires on **8** design-key/column pairs and it is NOT fixable — the design key genuinely cannot express `kb_sha256`, ARM-1 handles it at the read boundary, and no re-index changes it. It is reported every run as a `standing_condition` with its mitigation, and `ok` covers drift only. Failing CI daily on something nobody can fix is how a check gets switched off, which is the same outcome as not having it. 🔍 **One defect caught by measuring, not reasoning:** the first D11 built its partition map from `store.list_results()`, whose projection carries `elongation_model` but **not** `kb_sha256` or `operons` — so it silently checked one key of three and reported a clean corpus. **A probe that under-checks is worse than no probe, because it reads as verification.** It now reads the partitioning columns from the manifest and NAMES which it checked. *(original entry follows)* **`INVARIANTS.json` + B4 + B2 — the cheapest step, and the only one that reaches a direct Parquet reader.** Publish the invariant list machine-readably (loaded by the hygiene module, the HF dataset card and the MCP surface), ship the refusal-carrying fields as **real columns** (`provenance` is read-time-only today, `store.py:56-57`), and make `crashed` / `condition` non-nullable at the write path. Add the `integrity_check` probes **D10/D11/D12** in the same change — a declared invariant nobody verifies is a comment (`capability.py:19-22,595-633`). | A |
| **H-17b** | P1 · ✅ **DONE 2026-08-11 — boundary built, migration complete, registry reconciling.** *(This header read "HALF DONE — nothing is migrated onto it yet" through 2026-08-10; that is no longer true and the stale line was itself a small instance of the problem this item is about.)* **Final state:** 5 purposes; **9 call sites migrated** across batches 1-5; **35 read sites registered and reconciling both ways** (12 consumer + 23 direct); the detector rebuilt on the syntax tree and widened past `src/cellarium/` after it was found blind to `apps/server.py`; `scripts/check_read_sites.py` for a commit-time check. **Everything not migrated has a recorded decision**, not a backlog: 10 lookups that must NOT move (a purpose filter in front of a lookup would hide the row being looked up), 5 primitives that ARE the boundary, 13 maintenance and 3 aggregate sites that must read unfiltered, 1 `segments.repair` deferred on the filter-vs-contract question, and 2 `bespoke_projection` sites that stay until `rows()` grows a `columns=` argument. What remains for this thread is the [TOATTEMPT lint rule](#toattempt--the-banned-call-lint-rule-h-17bs-last-mile), scoped separately. *(original entry follows)* | `src/cellarium/hygiene.py`, 11 tests, three verified by injection. **The four purposes are real and MEASURABLY different on this corpus** — `analysis` 188 rows · `coverage` 418 (55 tombstoned) · `audit` 363 UN-deduped (36 duplicate rows, supersession visible) · `lethality` 363 including **127 non-reportable**. That 188-vs-363 gap is the whole point: a lethality question asked against the analysis set reads zero deaths for 127 runs. A `purpose` argument every branch answers identically would be decoration, and decoration that looks like a safeguard is worse than none — so the test that carries the weight is the one asserting the sets differ. **No default purpose:** `rows()` refuses and lists the four, because a default is how every caller ends up on `analysis`. **`ctx` names** every filter, the counts each changed, the invariants enforced (cited by INV id and CI-checked against `data/INVARIANTS.json`), the arm REFUSAL (narrowing is a refusal to pool, not a quiet filter), and — the field doing the real work — **`NOT_for`**, what this row set must not be used for. Built by composing the existing primitives (`survey.analysis_rows`, `store.list_results`, `audit._rows`) rather than re-querying: a fresh query would make this a FIFTH read path with its own subtly different filters, which is the defect and not the fix. ⏳ **MIGRATION IN SMALL VERIFIED BATCHES — 4 of the surface moved, and it is still the smaller half.** `hygiene.read_sites()` enumerates what the backlog said was never enumerated — and the FIRST count was wrong, in a way worth recording. It searched file TEXT, so it counted the tool's name inside the agent's system prompt and the `def` lines of the two primitives, reporting **15** consumer modules. Counted from the SYNTAX TREE there are **7**, against **8** modules that read the parquet directly. ⚠️ **That inverts a claim made twice in this entry: the consumer surface is the SMALLER one, not the larger.** The counter is now hybrid by necessity — a consumer is an `ast.Call`, but `read_parquet(` is DuckDB SQL inside an f-string and no syntax tree can see it as a call, so that half stays a string-literal scan with docstrings excluded. An instrument that over-counts reads as verification, which is what this whole item exists to end, so the instrument gets the same standard as the thing it measures. **Batch 1:** `rigor.disconfirm` (the site with the MEASURED 5.5x drift — the last place that should choose its own filters and now the first that cannot), `differential.matrix`, `launch.kb_dependents`. **Batch 4 (group A, zero behaviour change, each verified set-identical):** `resources._corpus_footprint` → `inventory` (it COUNTS, which is that purpose's contract), `manifest.integrity_check` → `inventory` (363 = 363 rows, ok=True unchanged), `corpus_schema.fmt` → `analysis` arm=all (236 = 236). **Batch 3:** `rigor.coverage` → `inventory`. **Batch 2:** `reconcile.corpus_identifiers` → `coverage`, because a mention of a TOMBSTONED run is still a mention of a real identifier and resolving it to nothing would classify it as prose. Each batch verified as SETS before and after (188 ids; 382 ids / 82 designs) and against the full suite. 🔎 **A GAP FOUND BY MIGRATING — and this is why it is done in batches: the four purposes did not cover every legitimate read.** ✅ **CLOSED by a FIFTH purpose, `inventory`** (batch 3): every live design, reportability-agnostic — the DENOMINATOR of *what fraction of the corpus have I examined?* `rigor.coverage` is migrated onto it; its denominator is unchanged at 77 designs. **It returns the SAME ROWS as `lethality`, and that is deliberate rather than duplication.** A purpose is a CONTRACT, not only a filter: `lethality` may be asked whether a design collapses and not for a channel mean; `inventory` may be COUNTED and not read at all. What must never happen is two purposes agreeing on both the filters and the contract, and `test_no_two_purposes_share_a_filter_set_AND_a_contract` pins that — verified by injection (give `inventory` `lethality`'s contract and it goes red). Borrowing the `lethality` name here would have been the cheaper fix and would have left the next reader of a coverage grid wondering why it reads the lethality set. **Also left alone deliberately:** the pure LOOKUPS (`hf`, `raw`, `trna`, `resources`, `segments` — find a run by id, count rows). A lookup is not a purpose-shaped read. **A test pins `migrated` as a SMALL fraction of the surface**, so this still cannot be closed by asserting progress: a boundary that exists is not a boundary that is used. ✅ **The DIRECT-read half is registered too (2026-08-11).** The registry claimed to cover "the read surface" while covering only the consumer half — the 8 modules issuing their own `read_parquet`, which bypass every rule in `data/INVARIANTS.json` rather than merely the boundary's filters, had no entries at all. Now 33 sites reconcile (10 consumer + 23 direct): 5 primitive, 13 maintenance, 3 aggregate, and **2 that are none of those**. 🔎 **`miase._rows` and `similarity._sim_rows` are BESPOKE PROJECTIONS** — ordinary readers going direct because no purpose projects the columns they need (`media_segments`; `growth_rate`/`species_panel`), each re-implementing `DEDUP_QUALIFY` and `_mark_dropped` BY HAND. That is the duplicated filtering that produced the measured 5.5x `disconfirm` drift, still live in two places. Filing them under `primitive` would have hidden the gap in a category meaning "cannot be fixed"; closing it needs either a wider projection or a `columns=` argument on `rows()`, which is a real change and not a batch tail. *(original entry follows)* **One read boundary — `hygiene.rows(purpose=…) -> (rows, ctx)`.** Purposes are **mandatory, not cosmetic**: `analysis` (deduped, reportable, live), `coverage` (+ tombstoned), `audit` (**un-deduped** — `audit._rows()` needs supersession visible, `audit.py:44`), and `lethality` (**non-reportable included** — collapse *is* the phenotype, `survey.py:244-250`). `ctx` names every filter applied and every refusal raised. Migrate the 7 `read_parquet` sites **and** the consumers downstream of `store.list_results()` / `analysis_rows()` (the true surface is larger than the 7 and was not enumerated). Ends drift of the kind already MEASURED — the `disconfirm` 5.5× interval. | A |
| **H-17c** | P2 | **Refusal at the boundary, scoped to the claim (`C` layered on `H-17b`'s `ctx`).** Refuse *"quote a mean"* below `MIN_SEEDS`/`MIN_GENERATIONS`; **allow** *"show me the rows"*. Reuses the shapes that exist (`support.attach()`, `capability.check()`). ⚠️ **Must never block a read.** The M-7 precedent is binding: a blocking gate parked 23/25 canonical questions. Also **NEEDS-DECISION:** whether `MIN_SEEDS=2`/`MIN_GENERATIONS=2` — chosen against this corpus's own target of 4 seeds — are the right floor for a **public** surface, where a third-party caller has no Cellwright loop to catch a thin claim. | A |
| **H-17d** | P2 · *with the production build* | **B3 — publish the unit of analysis (`lineage_id` + `generation_index`).** The only option that makes **independence** structurally unskippable, and the only one that cannot be retrofitted: it changes what a row **is**. Schedule it **with** the production corpus build, never as a metadata edit. Open: compatibility with the existing `per_generation` / `generation_qc` JSON columns is unexamined. | A |
| **H-17e** | P2 | **Close the two measured gaps.** (i) Add `kb_sha256` (and `operons`) to the `_deduped_rows` projection and make the partition tuple a **refusal** in the comparison path — → **`M-11`**, which owns the fix. (ii) **NOT ESTABLISHED and still open:** no tool refuses a cross-**MEDIUM** pooling. `one_factor_neighbours` requires the same `base` (`factors.py:256`), so the control-*selection* primitive honours the medium partition — but a hand-built cross-medium comparison meets **no refusal anywhere**, and `capability.py` refuses on the elongation axis only. Also unsettled: the canonical **key** for the medium axis, which is `condition` for most rows, `timeline` for 42/322, and `media_segments` for shift designs. | A |

⚠️ **Scope note, and the honest limit of this whole thread.** The production HF corpus schema **does not exist
yet**, so nothing here is validated against it — the placement design is written against the dev manifest's 47
columns and against the semantics that *should* survive a clean rebuild. Also **NOT verified**: whether `apps/`
reaches the manifest only through `store`/`survey` or has its own path; the `read_parquet` grep covered
`src/cellarium`, `evals` and `scripts` and found none outside `src/cellarium`, but a non-DuckDB route (e.g. a
cached JSON export) would not appear in that grep. *An absence I searched for is weaker evidence than a presence
I read.*

### G2 · Literature-validated science candidates (`wf_5cb5c805`, 2026-07-26)

Proposed by a 3-lens brainstorm, then each cross-checked against the literature. **None of these was in the
register before this pass** — recorded so the validation work does not evaporate. Status is the *literature's*
verdict, not an intention to build. The cross-cutting finding: **nothing here is methodologically novel** — each
is either a community norm we have not met or a solved method we were about to reinvent. That lowers the cost
(templates exist) and raises the bar (a reviewer knows the prior art).

| ID | Item + worked example | Literature verdict |
|----|----------------------|--------------------|
| ~~**SCI-TRNA-1**~~ | **✅ BUILT — `src/cellarium/trna.py` — then CORRECTED after a strict adversarial re-derivation overturned its original validation. Both corrections are the point of the row.** The corpus reported ONE `fraction_trna_charged` (~0.95); the raw listener is 86 tRNA species × timesteps, and the reader was averaging it away. Grouping into amino-acid families is still right and still ships. **① The headline validation was invalid.** `KO:argS` → arg 0.0 and `KO:pheS` → phe 0.0 were reported as blind recoveries. Those runs are **translationally ARRESTED**: `RibosomeData/effectiveElongationRate` is pinned at **0.0**, and the charged-fraction row mean has total variation **1.2e-07 / 1.4e-07** over the generation against **1.5e+00** for wild-type — seven orders of magnitude. The table is then `(86 − n_target)/86` exactly, computable from the KB isoacceptor count **with no simulation at all**. That third line rules out the convenient story — admitting only the 90%-knockdown dose still collapses the cluster, so this is not "the weak doses dragged it down"; graded knockdowns do not sit with full knockouts, which is a statement about perturbation TYPE, not dose. The confound gate cleared on its own as the corpus grew (**−0.227 → −0.082** at n=49), so measurement (a) below is now stale. **WHAT `passes=True` MUST NOT BE READ AS.** `acceptance()` now returns a `strength` block, and it is asserted: against an EXHAUSTIVE null over all 19,600 three-design subsets the cluster sits at **p=0.021** — real — but **6.1% of arbitrary triples clear the +0.30 gate**, and at n=49 the Fisher-z SE is **0.147**, so `|r| < 0.15` still asks a point estimate to be smaller than ~1 SE of itself. The durable claims are the p-value and the reduction (|r| 0.635 → 0.082), not the booleans; a reader quoting `passes` without `strength` overstates this. The `xfail(strict=True)` did exactly what it was built for: it XPASSed, turned CI red, and forced the marker to be deleted and the claim re-read. Also fixed en route: `cluster_null` enumerated C(n,k) exhaustively and **hung the suite** on a six-member cluster (15,890,700 subsets) — it now strides above a 50,000 cap, deterministically, because a safeguard that cannot be computed is not a safeguard. *(Original entry follows.)* **The severity-de-confounding gate now FAILS honestly and must be re-established, not restored.** With the no-op murA removed, `corr_growth_cos_to_wt = −0.238` — the double-centering no longer removes severity on the surviving cluster. Options, in preference order: (1) run genuine envelope-biosynthesis knockouts to replace murA and re-test (needs verified-applied KOs — use `scripts/verify_ko_applied.py` as a gate BEFORE admitting any run to the cluster); (2) re-derive the metric on the cluster that remains and report the honest correlation; (3) report the metric as not-yet-demonstrated. **Forbidden:** re-admitting murA or rpoB to make the number look better. Also worth checking whether `KO:rpoB` was propping up any other live gate the same way. **Update (CI-green pass, 2026-08):** the gate is now `xfail(strict=True)` in `tests/test_similarity.py` with the full evidence in the marker — **thresholds left at 0.15 / 0.30, deliberately unrelaxed**, and `strict` so a genuine re-establishment XPASSes and turns CI red rather than rotting into a silent skip. `acceptance()` now also reports `corr_growth_cos_to_wt_baseline` + `confound_abs_reduction` so the honest story is machine-readable. **Three new measurements to spend before option (1) is run.** (a) **The confound gate is below its own noise floor.** Live corpus is 44 designs × 199 species, 42 with growth: baseline (column-z only) **+0.536 → shipped −0.227**. At n=42 the Fisher-z SE is **0.160**, and a 500-draw species split-half puts the shipped confound at mean −0.207, **sd 0.175, range [−0.610, +0.171]** — it spans both signs. `|r| < 0.15` asks a point estimate to be smaller than one SE of itself, so **no** method reliably clears it at this corpus size. | **SCI-TRNA-5** | **Elf-style selective charging is OUT OF SCOPE for wcEcoli, and our 0.00e+00 is an IDENTITY rather than a measurement — verified in source.** Charging is solved as a 20-state ODE indexed by AMINO ACID; `polypeptide_elongation.py:163` then does `np.dot(fraction_charged, self.aa_from_trna)`, broadcasting each family's single scalar across its isoacceptor columns, and demand is split back across isoacceptors STRICTLY BY ABUNDANCE (`f_trna = n_trna / aa-totals`, ~line 717). Since charged fraction depends on the mismatch between an isoacceptor's demand share and its abundance share, allocating demand by abundance forces every family member to the same value **algebraically, in every condition**. Our measured within-family spread of exactly 0.00e+00 across leu(8)/arg(7)/ser(5) in starved, rich and minimal runs is that identity, not evidence about biology. **Verdict: CAN be extended (anticodons are already unused in `rnas.tsv`, abundances in `flat/trna_data/`, codons derivable from `sequence.fasta`) but SHOULD NOT be, next.** Cost 3-6 months / 400-600 CPU-hours, and adding `sim_data` fields changes `kb_sha256`, putting all ~297 corpus rows across a KB boundary. **Prior art:** Choi & Covert 2023 (PMC10325894) already built per-isoacceptor charging + codon elongation + wobble — check the Zenodo deposit (10.5281/zenodo.7859480) BEFORE any build; if usable, this verdict flips. **Also:** the proposed "spread >= 5x" gate is backwards — this class of fixed point drives non-exclusive isoacceptors to exactly zero (10^12x spread) whereas Dittmar observes a BOUNDED 5-10x with all values nonzero; Elf's own theory over-predicts retained isoacceptors 2-3x (Leu-4 predicted 0.8, observed 0.24). Target the bound, not the magnitude. | open (recommend NOT building) | The zero we measured is arithmetic, not biology |

Dong, Nilsson & Kurland (1996) measured ~44 SPECIES, not 86 genes, so these are an undocumented disaggregation. It matters quantitatively: the Leu ordering against Dittmar Table 1 flips from Spearman rho = **-0.10 to +0.90** purely on whether gene rows are SUMMED or AVERAGED when pooling to anticodon. **Next (2-3 days, worth doing even if we never extend):** reconcile anticodon-pooled totals against Dong 1996 under a SINGLE pooling rule declared BEFORE looking at any Dittmar comparison; pre-registered criterion = reproduce Dong per-species within stated error for >=90% of species. If it fails, the tRNA cistron expression used in every existing corpus run is arbitrary, which is a finding in its own right. | open | Declare the pooling rule before you look |

| ~~**EXT-PORT-1C**~~ | ~~**`getter._sequences` has no key for a polycistronic gene's own RNA id.**~~ **DONE.** The blocker and everything behind it had ONE root cause: **v3.0.1's code is self-consistent only with operons OFF**, where `rna_data` degenerates to one row per cistron. This tree runs operons ON, so `rna_data` is 3276 transcription units while the ported code indexes 4539 cistrons and 86 tRNAs. Fixed in `scripts/apply_trna_port.py` (now 24 idempotent steps) and verified by a new 14-second harness, `scripts/probe_relation.py`, which builds `SimulationDataEcoli` only as far as `Relation` off an existing `rawData.cPickle` with `relation.py` bind-mounted — no image rebuild per attempt. It also COUNTS the mRNA/protein warnings, because `_build_codon_sequences` only warns on a mismatch. ~~(a) `import warnings` and six other imports~~. ~~(b) cistron-vs-TU index space~~. ~~(c) the `[:-3]` compartment strip~~. ~~(d) cistron mRNA now parsed from each cistron's OWN GENE coordinates~~, the same construction `getter_functions.py:187-200` uses for monocistronic RNAs. **The obvious alternative — slicing the cistron out of a TU with `cistron_start_end_pos_in_tu` — was implemented, measured, and rejected.** It agrees for 4535 of 4539 cistrons but is wrong for the four whose genes overhang their TU's **5' end** (`wza`/G7107, `holC`/EG11413, `ytiC`/G0-16686, `dinG`/EG11357), because the assert at `transcription.py:823` guards only the 3' end. Head to head through `scripts/probe_relation.py`: **TU route 3 mismatching monomers, gene route 1.** (Along the way the TU route also needed `[start:end+1]` — the stored `end` is INCLUSIVE, and `[start:end]` leaves no stop codon so `find('*')` returns -1 and drops the last residue of EVERY protein.) ~~(e) multi-TU cistrons~~ — the TU route had to resolve them: 694 cistrons sit in more than one TU, 692 agree, and for the two that do not the lowest TU index is right for `EG11413_RNA` and **WRONG** for `G7107_RNA`. The gene route dissolves the question rather than answering it — a cistron has exactly one gene, so there is no TU to pick. ~~(f) the tRNA index space~~ — `free_trnas` now comes from `transcription.uncharged_trna_names`, the canonical 86-element cistron-space list. Taken from `rna_data` it was ~42 long, and `dict(zip(free_trnas, charged_trnas))` **truncates to the shorter without raising**, so it would have paired the wrong tRNAs silently. Everything downstream was already aligned to that list: `charged_trna_names`, the 86 columns of `aa_from_trna`, `initiator_trnas`, the six hard-coded wobble tRNAs, and the `K_M` keys in the ported kinetics tables. `trnas_to_codons` now comes out **(63, 86)** instead of (63, ~42). The anticodon is read from `raw_data.rnas` because it is a column of `rnas.tsv` that reaches neither `cistron_data` nor `rna_data`. ~~(g) monomers whose mRNA cannot translate to their protein~~ — **one**, `PHNE-MONOMER`. (`EG11357-MONOMER` and `MONOMER0-4391` were also mismatches under the TU route and are correct under the gene route; that measurement is what decided between them.) Checked against v3.0.1's own `rnas.tsv`/`genes.tsv`/`proteins.tsv`: identical protein sequences, identical coordinates, identically EMPTY `coding_segments`, so this is a knowledge-base limitation both trees share and not a port defect. v3.0.1 only avoids phnE by typing `EG11283_RNA` `'pseudo'` — the single row whose type differs between the trees. They are RECORDED on `sim_data.relation.codon_sequence_mismatches` and excluded from the codon->amino-acid accumulation rather than dropped, because dropping leaves a protein the kinetic model can never elongate, and the harness allowlists exactly these three so a fourth still fails. ~~(h) `np.bool`~~, removed in NumPy 1.24 against the image's 1.26.3 — two uses, and the one in `KineticTrnaChargingModel.__init__` is executed by neither ParCa nor a default simulation, so it would have fired at the start of the first `--kinetic-trna-charging` campaign. | done | one root cause: v3.0.1 assumed operons OFF |
| **EXT-PORT-4** | **~~ParCa end-to-end~~, then the structure comparison that a green run does NOT give you.** ~~ParCa is GREEN — `rc=0`, 413 s, `simData.cPickle` written, zero tracebacks.~~ What that does NOT establish is content — so it was checked, by four agents plus an adversarial pass. **`trnas_to_codons` (63, 86) is VERIFIED**: rebuilt from scratch with a hard-coded NCBI table-11, an independent antiparallel-pairing function, and anticodons keyed by tRNA NAME (so the test does not assume column order) — element-for-element identical, 153 nonzeros, and 0 of 200 random column permutations reproduce it, so the test genuinely discriminates order rather than width. All 153 assignments are explained: 80 Watson-Crick, 32 G34:U3, 27 U34:G3, 8 inosine-34 (argQ/V/Y/Z), 2 lysidine-34 (ileX, ileY), 4 initiator-to-`start`; **zero unexplained**. No all-zero rows or columns. **The genetic-code check, by contrast, was REFUTED as circular** and the finding is worth keeping: `codons_to_amino_acids` is not compared against a table, it is DERIVED by translating each mRNA with `Bio.Seq.translate()` (the standard table) and discarding every monomer whose translation disagrees with its curated protein — so agreement with NCBI table 11 is guaranteed by construction. What the check does legitimately establish: every column sums to exactly 1, the matrix is {0,1}, UGA maps to `L-SELENOCYSTEINE[c]` and nothing else, `start` is index 0 and maps to MET, and only 3 of ~1.3M codon positions deviate from the standard table — all three the hard-coded `coding_opal` selenoproteins. Remaining here: compare against v3.0.1's own built structures rather than against first principles. Then compare `sim_data.relation` against v3.0.1 by SHAPE **and CONTENT** — current shapes are `codons` 63 (62 sense + start; UGA kept for selenocysteine), `trnas_to_codons` (63, 86), `codons_to_amino_acids` (21, 63), `residue_weights_by_codon` (63,), `trna_codon_pairs` 153, `_codon_sequences` 4310. Shape agreement is necessary and NOT sufficient — a (63, 86) matrix with the columns permuted has the right shape and the wrong biology. | open (next action) | shape is not content |

| **EXT-PORT-12** | **The calibration question, answered against the papers — and the answer is NOT "refit ppGpp".** Hypothesis (C), a convention artifact, is **dead**: both models compute `charged/(charged+free)` over the identical 86 bulk species, neither carries a ternary-complex or ribosome-bound pool, and both aggregate to the same 21-vector before `ppgpp_metabolite_changes` sees anything. Like-for-like at identical state: **0.9795 vs 0.8295**; selenocysteine is worth 0.002 and the initiator/elongator split ~0.01. **The measurements** (Avcilar-Kucukgoze et al. 2016, *NAR* 44(17):8324, doi:10.1093/nar/gkw697 — M9 + 0.4% glucose, 37 °C, doubling 43.3 min, i.e. essentially our basal condition): charging **oscillates around 50–60%**, by periodate-protection microarray corroborated with acid-urea northerns. So **0.979 is outside every measurement** and 0.830 sits at the high end of the band. The familiar "80–90%" is an extrapolation from three papers (Yegian 1966; Varshney 1991, a METHOD paper on mutant initiator tRNAs; Sørensen 2001), which those authors quote and then explicitly reject. **Choi & Covert's own published aggregate is 78.8%**, validated against four datasets not used for parameterisation — our build gives 0.830, four points high, which is itself worth explaining. **CAUTION ON OUR HEADLINE RESULT:** measured within-family spread is between ~0.00 (Avcilar-Kucukgoze: "all tRNA isoacceptors within one family were charged to similar levels") and 0.16 (Dittmar et al. 2005, *EMBO Rep* 6(2):151, doi:10.1038/sj.embor.7400341 — five Leu isoacceptors at 0.80/0.77/0.68/0.76/0.84 unperturbed). **Our kinetic run gives LEU 0.25 and GLY 0.32 — roughly twice the widest measurement.** The capability is real; the magnitudes are NOT validated. **Decisive context:** Choi & Covert list ppGpp integration as future work in as many words — "Integrating this work with a model of growth rate control via ppGpp ... would give us the opportunity to explore overall growth responses in amino acid-limited media" — so **nobody has run this combination**, and there is no published calibration to inherit. | open | the 98% figure was never measured |
| ~~**SYNTH-1**~~ | ~~**The 1.29x synthetase excess.**~~ **RESOLVED, and it dissolves the aggregate gap.** Three things were wrong with how I framed it. **(a) The reference is not a measurement.** 1.29x is against `optimization/trna_synthetase_dynamic_range.tsv`, whose own header says it was built from *sample simulations* — 20 generations x 5 seeds of the TRANSLATION-SUPPLY model with tRNA charging OFF. And the v3.0.1 authors flagged this prospectively in that same header: ParCa estimates "differed from simulations enough to warrant gathering these data from sample simulation runs." **(b) 1.29 is the operons-OFF number.** Our production operons-ON KB is **1.17**. **(c) It is not uniform** — the range is 0.651 (TYRS) to 1.472 (ALAS), with two synthetases BELOW the reference. Control: it is synthetase-specific, not a volume convention — under the identical convention tRNA matches v3.0.1 to 0.7% and amino-acid targets to exactly 1.000. **THE ACTUAL FINDING: it is an initial-state TRANSIENT that self-corrects by division.** Synthetase ratio 1.242 (t=0) -> 1.007 (end of generation), and the charged fraction falls with it: 0.8388 (t=10 s) -> **0.7845 (end)**, drift -0.0543. **So the '1.29x excess' and the '-0.052 drift' were never two factors — they are one.** And the end-of-generation aggregate of **0.7845 sits against Choi & Covert's published 0.788** — the 0.042 gap I had been citing was an artifact of measuring at 120 s. Caveats kept deliberately: the causal link is time-confounded (r=0.986 but both trend with t), it is one seed and one generation, and we do not know at what depth 0.788 was reported. | done | one transient, not two factors |

Arithmetic verified: 26/(1 + 1/3.33) = 20.0 s⁻¹ exactly, with 3.33 µM being one isoacceptor's share of a 10 µM family split three ways. **That is a published precedent, by the authors of the rate law, for exactly our 21→86 operation, and it says: leave K_rta alone and re-pin `k_el^max`.** | done | the precedent is the authors' own |

| **UNIFY-1** | **The PARITY model — rationale, scope, and why this direction.** **What it solves:** the two elongation models each represent what the other structurally cannot. Steady-state has the stringent response (RelA/SpoT making ppGpp from charging state) but solves charging as a ~20-state ODE indexed by AMINO ACID and broadcasts it across all 86 isoacceptors, so within-family spread is 0.000 by construction. Kinetic has 86 independent isoacceptors and codon-level elongation but mentions ppGpp **zero** times against steady-state's thirty; under it metabolism pins ppGpp at a constant and transcription regulates off a frozen pool, silently. **Neither can run the experiment that matters** — Elf's selective-charging theory predicts an isoacceptor PARTITION under amino-acid limitation, which needs isoacceptor resolution AND a working starvation response at the same time. Choi & Covert name this as future work in as many words. **WHY THIS DIRECTION and not "add isoacceptors to steady-state":** the two are not symmetric. ppGpp is a **bolt-on** — `ppgpp_metabolite_changes` is a module-level FREE FUNCTION over concentrations, and the kinetic model already computes every one of its 15 arguments at equal or finer resolution. Isoacceptor resolution is **structural** — you cannot split charging demand among isoacceptors of one amino acid without knowing which CODONS are being read, which requires the codon reading matrix, the codon sequences and codon-level elongation. Adding that to the steady-state model does not extend it; it rebuilds the kinetic model. The steady-state model's current split is 'in proportion to abundance', which IS the assumption that yields zero spread. **Scope:** 4 bulk-molecule views + 2 param dicts; a request block; an evolve block; an exact 86→21 partition sum **at the ppGpp interface ONLY** (the elongation model stays at 86 throughout, so the Elf observable is fully retained); plus three coupling fixes. **Three silent-failure modes, each measured:** (i) the ppGpp block must be LAST in evolve — views are per-PROCESS, so it reads the same ATP cell earlier lines already decremented; placed earlier it permits synthesis the cell cannot pay for and the error surfaces against whichever OTHER process merges first; (ii) `f` must come from `monomerUsages`, not `aas_used` — the latter is amino acids consumed by CHARGING, not incorporated at the ribosome; same shape, same magnitude, wrong quantity, and RelA would sense charging demand instead of A-site occupancy, which is exactly what it exists to sense; (iii) `counts_to_molar` must be computed once at request and reused at evolve, or cell mass differs between the two and parity is silently broken. **`np.prod` clarification:** RelA's term multiplies across the 21 amino acids. Parity and steady-state both feed it 21 values, so this is NOT a defect of parity — it is the reason the codon-resolved version cannot be reached by aggregating first, i.e. why the two are distinct models rather than approximations of each other. **Fitting:** parity needs NO structural refit — same quantity, same units, same resolution as what the ppGpp parameters were fitted against, and the aggregation is exact. But charging differs in VALUE (~0.785 kinetic vs ~0.976 steady-state), so ppGpp may come out different with correct plumbing. That is a FORK to observe, not a licence to refit: either 0.785 is better and the ppGpp parameters were calibrated against an over-charged pool, or the kinetic charging is wrong. Decide after running, never before. | open (next action) | ppGpp bolts on; isoacceptors do not |

Also established: >100% values are real in that dataset (ArgUCU ~131%, GluUUC ~129% in M9) and incomplete periodate oxidation **cannot** produce them — the bias is strictly upward and asymptotes AT 100%, so those require a separate normalisation error. | ~~**EXT-PORT-11**~~ | ~~**Cellarium cannot select an elongation model at all.**~~ **DONE — the axis exists end to end, and it is expressible even though no kinetic run has been made.** `Design.elongation_model` is a **string**, not a bool (`steady_state`/`kinetic`/`coarse_kinetic`, and the set will grow), validated against `capability.ELONGATION_MODES` so an unknown value is refused at construction and again at `envelope.check` rather than mapping to no flag and silently running the default model. It **defaults to `steady_state`**, which is load-bearing rather than convenient: every historical `design.json` is validated back through this class by `runner._evacuate` and `manifest._design_from_dir`, and without the default `run_one` would RAISE on the existing corpus. `runner._variant_args` maps it to exactly one runSim option and **emits nothing for the default**, so every pre-existing command line is byte-identical. **Three sites were load-bearing and all three failed silently.** (1) `_variant_index`/`_run_subpath`: a plain KO carries an explicit index and no timeline, so it bypassed BOTH the content hash and `_needs_distinct_dir` — a kinetic and a steady-state KO of one gene resolved to ONE directory, and wcEcoli `rmtree`s its output dir before every run, so this **destroys data rather than mislabelling it** (the SCI-TRNA-4 leu-arm race on a new axis). Now a `__el<mode>` discriminator, and `_needs_distinct_dir` is defined in terms of the same `_dir_discriminator` so predicate and path cannot drift. (2) `manifest._design_tag` appends `#elong:<mode>` — separator chosen because `·`/`/` split labels, `\|` is the compound-dose form and `@` already appends media — which propagates to `label`, `design_key`, `count_runs`' prefix, the **deterministic crash-row id**, and via `survey.design_tag` to every analysis grouping. That id is where the dedup key actually collided: a successful row's id is a uuid4, but `_crash_row`'s is `sha256(perturbation\|design_tag\|seed)`, so two arms produced one `(id, path)` pair and `ts DESC` silently superseded one. Pinned in `tests/test_elongation_axis.py` on **both halves of the key**, so narrowing it later still fails loudly. (3) the read layer: `manifest.elongation_sql()` resolves the column presence-aware — `COALESCE(…, 'steady_state')` once a shard carries it, the literal before — so `store.list_results` (no tier fallback: naming a missing column RAISES) and `survey._deduped_rows` (which silently DOWNGRADES past a Binder Error, the `machine`-column incident) both land in the same change as the value. Also threaded: `_flat_row` **and** the crash row's hand-written dict (the rows that most need to name the attempted model were the only ones without the column), `factors.parse`/`identity`/`one_factor_neighbours` (D4 would otherwise **invert** and argue to merge the two arms as aliases), the approval airlock (`launch.propose`→`approve_and_run`, so what a human approves and what executes cannot differ), `council._DESIGN_KEYS`, `audit._design`, and `generate.py --elongation` + a first paired kinetic arm. `backfill_elongation_model()` implements decision 4 and is **written but not executed** — reads already resolve correctly, and stamping is a corpus rewrite for a human to run. Two new invariants: D8 (no NULL elongation once the column exists) and D9 (label tag vs stored column). **On the ParCa question raised here:** left as one `kb_sha256`, since the kinetic model reads `sim_data.relation` from the same knowledge base; the refusal text already states such a campaign is not poolable with the existing corpus. | done | a shared output dir destroys data, it does not mislabel it |

| **PARCA-1** | **The fur/tnaC swing is not a phnE1 story — it exposed two live defects in ParCa, and one is a plain arithmetic error.** ~~Both of my hypotheses were wrong~~ and the truth is worse than either. ~~**(1) `fast_nonnegative_least_squares.py` solved a 1x1 NNLS block as `x = max(0, b)` when the answer is `max(0, b/a)`**~~ — **FIXED.** It now divides by the coefficient, with a `_DUST = 1e-12` guard: the matrix is rank-deficient by 214 of 3166 columns, so `a` is routinely round-off from an earlier normalisation rather than a coefficient, and dividing by dust is what produced the 4e13 /s rates the caller then clipped to a plausible-looking number. Verified against `scipy.optimize.nnls` as an independent oracle: **0 of 400** randomised cases disagree; `min‖2x−4‖` now gives 2.0 where the old code gave 4.0; a 1e-16 column gives 0.0 rather than 3e16. ~~A ParCa rebuild is the acceptance test~~ — **RUN: green, 411 s, 0 tracebacks.** What it changed: **11 of 3276 TUs by >1%, 8 by >10%**, the largest being `TU0-44217[c]` 4.680 → 2.285 min (104.8%) and `TU0-44215[c]` 11.161 → 5.718 min (95.2%) — ~2x corrections, exactly matching the predicted 13 blocks with `a != 1`, worst 2.048x. **What it did NOT change, and this matters: the saturation is untouched.** 244 TUs sit on the 91.2-min floor before AND after; 7 on the 0.4-min ceiling; median 5.191 min in both. `fur` is still 91.200, `tnaC` still 0.400, `trpS` still 91.200. So the arithmetic defect is fixed and the SATURATION defect is not — they were always two separate problems and only the smaller one is closed. — it never divides by the coefficient. Verified by reading it. Correct only when `a == 1`; measured, 13 blocks have `a != 1`, worst 2.048x. Live in the shipped KB. **(2) The estimator SATURATES rather than fits for ~8% of transcription units.** 91.2 min is not a fitted half-life, it is a hard floor: `min_deg_rates[is_mRNA] = mRNA_cistron_deg_rates.min()`, and any TU whose NNLS coefficient returns exactly 0 is assigned exactly that floor. **244 of 3026 fitted TUs in the new KB and 245 in the OLD (shipped) one sit bit-exactly on it.** The half-life distribution jumps from p90 = 11.0 min straight to p95 = p99 = p100 = 91.2 min; of the 260 TUs slower than 20 min, 244 (93.8%) ARE the floor. **And the floor rests on ONE cistron** — `shoB` (G0-10634_RNA), whose source row in Chen et al. 2014 is `shoB,1235,319,1,131.522,0`: **total fragments = 1, StdDev = 0**, with no coverage filter in the converter (211 of 847 rows have a single fragment). Second-slowest is 32.4 min, so shoB is 2.8x the next value out of 847. **phnE1 is not special:** 7 of 13 arbitrary single-cistron removals produced the same 20-228x swings, and two (`tpr`, `tdcR`) reproduce the fur/tnaC flip digit-for-digit. Root cause: the NNLS is rank-deficient by 214 of 3166 columns, and the swings are ~1e-16 round-off being divided into, yielding 4e13 /s rates that the clip at :736-737 silently rewrites to "the fastest measured mRNA in E. coli". **Corpus: untouched** — 0 of 322 rows are on the new KB. `TU0-1283` (fur) has expression 5e-20 and a count of 0 at every timestep, so that particular 228x lands on a TU the model never transcribes (Fur protein moves +6.3%) — but that reassurance does NOT generalise: `trpS` `TU877[c]` carries **25.47% of its gene's expression** on the 91.2-min floor. Protective fact: all 58 rRNA/tRNA TUs use the stable-RNA constant, so **tRNA abundances are untouched by any of this**. | open (blocks EXT-PORT-13) | a 1x1 NNLS block that forgot to divide |

| ~~**EXT-PORT-16**~~ ✅ (necessary conditions) | **DONE 2026-08-08 — and it found one on its first run.** `tests/test_agent_elongation_axis.py`, 13 tests. **Scope, stated rather than implied:** whether the model CHOOSES to route a question or remembers to name the mode in prose is model behaviour needing a live call, and `tests/test_agent.py` mocks the client entirely while this repo has no live-gated agent-test convention — a live test here would be one nobody runs. What IS deterministic is the two things that make ignoring the axis possible: **(1) the INSTRUCTION** — does `agent.SYSTEM` still teach the axis (all three modes, what the shared column means under each, and the never-pool RULE); **(2) the EVIDENCE** — does every mode-sensitive tool NAME the model it answered under, does `survey_corpus` disclose which arm it ranked, does every listed run carry its mode, and does a refusal survive `_truncate_tool_result`, the single funnel through which every tool result enters the model's context. An agent cannot respect an axis it is never told about, nor report a mode that never reaches it. Necessary conditions, no API key, and neither was covered.
  - **Found on the first run: `selective_charging` named the elongation model in its REFUSAL branch and not in its SUCCESS branch** — i.e. everywhere except where it returns numbers. It is the tool the agent calls for the headline selectivity question, and its `per_family` values are 86 independent measurements under kinetic and one value broadcast 86 times under steady_state, so a `drop_pct` without its mode is a number whose meaning cannot be recovered. Fixed.
  - **And a false-pass in my own test, caught by injection.** `"not pool" in SYSTEM` passed because it matched the PARCA-3 sentence about a rebuild's runs being "not poolable with the corpus" — a rule about ARMS, not about elongation. The assertion is now scoped to the passage that teaches the axis. Both injections (drop the mode from `selective_charging`; strip the rule from the prompt) now fail the suite. *(Original entry follows.)* **Nothing tests that CELLWRIGHT behaves correctly on the new axis.** `grep` for `elongation_model` across `tests/` hits `test_capability.py`, `test_elongation_axis.py` and `test_trna.py` — and NOT `test_agent.py` or `test_council.py`. So the axis is pinned at the plumbing level (Design -> CLI args -> dedup key -> manifest row) and at the registry level, and **not at the level where it actually matters**: does the agent route a ppGpp question away from kinetic mode, does it refuse an isoacceptor question against steady-state runs, does it mention the mode when reporting a number, and does it pick the right mode when asked a question only one model can answer. The registry can be perfectly correct and the agent can still ignore it — that gap is the entire reason `test_agent.py` exists for the other capabilities. Needs the same treatment here. | open (next action) | the registry is tested; the reader of it is not |

| **EXT-PORT-7** | **Loose ends found while getting here, each small and each real.** ~~(a) the single Biopython `Partial codon` warning during `Relation`~~ — **RESOLVED, not a fourth thing**: it is `EG11283_RNA` (phnE1) at 839 nt, `len % 3 == 2`, already one of the three known mismatches. Traced to the `.translate()` in `_build_codon_sequences`; exactly one such warning fires. (b) **`transcription.py:823` asserts only that a cistron does not overhang its TU's 3' end**, so the four pairs that overhang the 5' end (`wza`/G7107, `holC`/EG11413, `ytiC`/G0-16686, `dinG`/EG11357) carry silently wrong start/end offsets. This is a PRE-EXISTING defect in this tree, not something the port introduced, but the port now depends on it: codon sequences come from gene coordinates while the runtime places ribosomes with `cistron_start_end_pos_in_tu` (`initialization.py:1164`), so for those four the two disagree. Matching the wrong offsets deliberately was the alternative and was rejected — it would mean shipping a sequence measured to be wrong. Fix the assert to guard both ends. (c) `codons_to_trnas` is listed as a probe marker in `capability.py` but is a local variable in `relation.py`, not an attribute — the marker passes on a substring match, which is looser than intended. ~~(d) two silent-wrong conditions the ported asserts leave open~~ — **CLOSED.** The overloaded-codon assert catches a codon mapped to two amino acids but not one mapped to NONE: the mapping is derived empirically by observing (codon, amino acid) pairs across the proteome, so an unused codon yields an empty list, an all-zero column, and then an `IndexError` from the residue-weight loop with no hint of the cause. And that loop **crosses two amino-acid orderings** — a row index into `codons_to_amino_acids` (ordered by `molecule_groups.amino_acids`) used to index `translation_monomer_weights` (ordered by `amino_acid_code_to_id_ordered.values()`). They are element-equal today, and nothing enforced it; had they diverged every residue weight would have been silently PERMUTED with no error anywhere. Both now asserted with messages that name the cause. | open | small, real, and easy to lose |

| **EXT-4** | **⚠️ The tRNA abundance deviation does NOT wash out at the resolution the model reports — CORRECTED with the authoritative anticodon map.** The defence of EXT-3 was that per-gene misassignment is harmless because charging is aggregated per amino acid. Measured, and false. **Superseded numbers:** a first pass mapped genes to amino acids by NAME PREFIX (`leuP`->Leu), leaving 7 of 86 genes unattributed and reporting median 0.885, 8/20 within ±25%, rho 0.854. `rnas.tsv` field 8 carries each gene's **anticodon** — already in our tree, read by nothing — which identifies the species unambiguously and covers **86/86** genes. Recomputed on that basis (anticodon -> codon -> amino acid): <br><br>`AA     wcEcoli   Dong    ratio  n`<br>`Ala      0.680   0.770   0.883  5`<br>`Arg      1.180   1.330   0.887  7`<br>`Asn      0.425   0.240   1.771  4`<br>`Asp      0.408   0.480   0.851  3`<br>`Cys      0.172   0.320   0.539  1`<br>`Gln      0.497   0.330   1.508  4`<br>`Glu      0.740   0.940   0.787  4`<br>`Gly      0.647   1.300   0.497  6`<br>`His      0.160   0.130   1.231  1`<br>`Ile      0.506   0.690   0.734  3`<br>`Leu      0.929   1.820   0.510  8`<br>`Lys      0.675   0.380   1.776  6`<br>`Met      1.542   0.520   2.966  8`<br>`Phe      0.269   0.210   1.281  2`<br>`Pro      0.655   0.440   1.489  3`<br>`Sec      0.190   0.040   4.750  1`<br>`Ser      0.722   0.760   0.951  5`<br>`Thr      0.794   0.530   1.498  4`<br>`Trp      0.063   0.190   0.333  1`<br>`Tyr      0.600   0.400   1.500  3`<br>`Val      0.993   1.030   0.964  7`<br><br>**median 0.964 | range 0.333-4.750 | only 6 of 21 within ±25% | Spearman rho 0.816 (p=7e-6).** The conclusion SURVIVES but its content changed: the median is now essentially 1.0 (total mass is conserved, as EXT-3 established) while the SPREAD is wider, and the offenders are different. **Sec 4.750x** is the largest deviation and was entirely invisible before (`selC` was unmapped). **Met 2.966x** on 8 genes (worse than the 2.236x reported). **Lys 0.671 -> 1.776** because `RNA0-301/302/303` are all lysine tRNAs. **Val 0.809 -> 0.964** because `RNA0-300` is a valine tRNA that was missed. So the amino-acid tRNA pools the model actually uses deviate from their cited source by 0.33-4.75x, affecting EXISTING results and not only a future per-isoacceptor port. **Remaining caveats:** Dong Table 3 pools `Gly1+2` and `Ile1+2`, and several of his species share an anticodon (Met f1/f2/m and Ile2 all CAU; Thr1/Thr3 both GGU; Tyr1/Tyr2 both GUA), so amino-acid totals are sound but per-species attribution within those groups is still ambiguous. **Next:** decide whether to correct the flat file — which changes `kb_sha256` corpus-wide — or document the deviation as a known model-input limitation and carry it as an error bar into EXT-PORT-3. | open | Recomputed on the anticodon column; every earlier number superseded |
| **EXT-5** | **Combine Dong with modern absolute methods — but only if EXT-4 confirms the deviation.** Dong Table 2 already gives absolute molecules-per-cell AND % of total tRNA per species at 0.4 doublings/h, so the slowest growth rate needs no disaggregation at all — use Table 2 directly. What no modern source appears to provide is the per-isoacceptor GROWTH-RATE series that wcEcoli's five columns require. Proposed split: take per-species abundance from a modern absolute method (mim-tRNAseq, OTTER, AQRNA-seq, nanopore), anchor to Dong Table 2 at 0.4, and use Dong Table 3 only for the SHAPE across growth rates — separating "how much" (modern, better) from "how it scales" (only Dong has it). **The join mechanism is now settled:** `rnas.tsv` field 8 (anticodon) maps all 86 genes to species with full coverage, so distributing any per-species measurement onto wcEcoli genes needs no new curation — this was previously unspecified. **Unverified prerequisite:** that any modern dataset actually covers E. coli K-12 at defined growth rates. Confirm coverage BEFORE committing to this design. | open | Table 2 is directly usable; only the scaling needs Dong |

### G4 · "The Well, for the Cell" at scale — open problems to revisit when the corpus grows

From the codegraph audit (`wf_c6e5b391`, 2026-07-26). Every NO-GO in that audit was **corpus-shape-dependent**,
and the shape is going to change. Recorded so the verdicts get re-opened on the right trigger rather than
inherited as permanent. **The trigger is SHAPE, not SIZE:** the current corpus is a *star* — single-factor
perturbations off one control, diameter 2, zero designs crossing two factors — so every "multi-hop" query
terminates in one hop at a constant. Nothing here becomes true merely because the corpus got bigger.

| ID | Open problem | Trigger to re-open | Notes |
|----|-------------|--------------------|-------|
| ~~**WELL-1**~~ | **✅ DONE — declared factor schema, folded together with WELL-6m and the relabelling, because they were one problem.** `src/cellarium/factors.py`. **(a) Factors, typed** — `parse()` decomposes a design key into `family / base / factor / level_raw / level_num / genes`, pure and I/O-free. Every one of the 60 corpus designs parses; `0.6x`→0.6, `4op`→4, and the awkward `kin_w:1e-7_default`→1e-7 (the suffix is real — it marks the model's own default). `genes` is an order-normalised SET, so set-membership and dose-response stay in different fields. Works retroactively on every existing row: **no Docker, no re-index.** **(b) Identity, not name** (WELL-6m) — `canonical_id` for a knockout is built from the **ko_index set**, because that is the experiment the simulator runs. `KO:pheS` and `KO:thrS` now collapse to one id, so an alias can never be counted as an independent replicate; `dedupe()` reports any group that is secretly one run, and a CI test fails if the shipped corpus ever contains one. For a single-gene design the co-addressed genes are true `aliases`; for a multi-KO they are only `same_index_genes`, which is a weaker claim and is not conflated. **(c) Honest names** — every design carries `label_integrity` (`ok` / `operon_wide` / `misnamed` / `relabel_required` / `no_design_possible`) and a `true_label`: `KO:flgB`→`operon_KO:flgBCDEFGHIJ`, `KO:rpoB`→`TU_KO:rplKAJL-rpoBC(silences nothing measured)`, the triple→`gene_KO:gltX+spoT(relA NOT silenced)`, `rRNA_KO:4op`→`rRNA_operons_removed:4of7`. **(d) Wired where it matters** — `survey_corpus` is the mandatory first read, so any non-`ok` design now carries its true label *with the number*, rather than being discoverable only if the agent thinks to ask. Six shipped designs are flagged there today. **(e) `one_factor_neighbours()`** is the correct-control query (WELL-2) as a plain filter over typed fields, returned in dose order. 21 tests. | done | Three problems, one schema |

The manifest **row is never touched**. `reason` is required (an unrecorded drop is the silent loss this prevents); it **refuses an ambiguous id** that maps to >1 physical run (the data-loss shape the dedup key exists to stop); idempotent. Surveys mark `_dropped` and **exclude it from ranking + comparisons + lethality, but coverage KEEPS it** (`n_runs_dropped` + `dropped_runs` with reasons) — the DB never silently forgets. `manifest.dedup_key_py` mirrors the SQL `DEDUP_KEY` and is **pinned equal on every corpus path** (a mismatch would make a drop silently no-op). Shipping this at 10⁶ runs would return confident, biologically meaningless neighbours. | — (already run) | **This is the argument for acting early**: one query, on a corpus small enough to check by hand. At scale the same failure is undetectable. |

Envelope-biosynthesis KOs (fabI/lpxC/murA/glmS) **separate**: within +0.206 vs rest +0.111. Dose series hold: glc_2mM↔glc_5mM +1.000, rRNA_KO:4op↔6op +0.927, acetate↔succinate +0.984. **What still fails:** `rpmE`↔`rpmJ` (both 50S ribosomal proteins) do **not** pair — both go to `no_oxygen`; the ppGpp pair is asymmetric (2.0x→1.6x, but 1.6x→`KO:dapA`); and `minus_magnesium`/`succinate` act as **generic attractors**, suggesting a residual "generic stress" first component. **Next, in order:** remove PC1 before comparing; try protein rather than mRNA (`kind='protein'`); weight genes by cross-seed reliability; and re-run once WELL-1 lets us hold condition fixed. **Acceptance bar for shipping: ≥4/5 on the pair scorecard AND rpmE↔rpmJ pairing.** | metric work now; index only at ≥10⁴ runs | The negative-then-partial result IS the deliverable — this is what testing early buys |

That is true only of the **design-derivation** graph, and only partly — 37 of 60 designs are spokes off `wildtype/basal`, but the other 23 sit on five monotone dose **ladders** (`kin_w` 8 levels, `sec_pen` 5, `ppgpp_conc` 4, `rRNA_KO` 3, glucose 3) with chain diameter up to 7, so "diameter 2" was wrong. The claim also silently quantified over graphs it never examined.)* **What actually holds, per graph:** design-derivation → star for the spokes, and for the ladders a path embedding just recovers dose *order*, which WELL-1 makes a literal column; `pathway_panel` bipartite → **measured to be a disjoint union of 13 stars, 199 symbols, zero shared between pathways** — provably the one-hot the manifest already has; TF/regulon → **zero of the 60 designs attach** (none of the 24 KO'd genes has `is_tf=true`; only 23 of 4,724 genes are TFs), an architectural ceiling no corpus growth relieves; raw iML1515 → currency-hub collapse (`h_c` degree 1,079), the failure Ma & Zeng documented in 2003 (PMID 12538249); **currency-pruned iML1515 → genuinely NOT a star** (diameter 9, mean path 3.03) and carrying real graded mechanism — this is where the old ruling over-reached — but killed by measurement rather than argument: exact all-pairs shortest path over 2,712 nodes runs in **0.6 s**, so there is nothing to compress, and head-to-head the FBA threshold rule alone scored **MCC 0.780** vs **0.397** for a 16-dim spectral embedding, with FBA+topology adding **ΔMCC +0.010, 95% CI [−0.026, +0.046]** — containing zero. **The generalisable lesson: on every non-star graph here, the embedding lost to an exact computation over the same object.** Use graph *distance*, never a learned graph *embedding*. GEARS-style cold start (Roohani 2024, doi:10.1038/s41587-023-01905-6) is gated on data we do not have — zero evaluable combination cells — and at 800× our scale Ahlmann-Eltze 2025 (PMID 40759747) found nothing beat a simple additive model for unseen doubles. Skeptical anchor: Brouard, Mourad & Vialaneix 2024, *Should we really use graph neural networks for transcriptomic prediction?* (PMID 38349060). | a non-star graph where exact computation is measurably too slow — not yet true of any of them | Default **no**, now on evidence rather than assertion |
