| ~~**WELL-6g**~~ ✅ | **⚠️ CORRECTNESS — validated on REAL simOut, and my own first two rules were both wrong.** *(Two superseded claims, kept because both were briefly shipped: (i) "a KO silences the whole operon" — right for some designs, backwards for others; (ii) "a gene is silenced IFF n_tu == 1, validated 27/27" — **OVERFIT to three genes**. Over 41 measurements it is 40/41, refuted by `KO:dapA` fully silencing `bamC` at n_tu=2.)* **Mechanism, code-traced and PROVEN:** `gene_knockout.py` → `adjust_final_expression([i],[0])` → zeroes `rna_synth_prob[i]`/`rna_expression[i]`, indexed over `rna_data`, whose rows are **transcription units** (`transcription.py:531`) plus orphan cistrons. One TU is zeroed. **Hinges entirely on operons-ON** (`DEFAULT_OPERON_OPTION='on'`, `knowledge_base_raw.py:188`); with `--operons off` every row is a cistron and none of this applies — the corpus was built ON (`variant_map.json` holds literal TU ids, 3,276 rows < 4,724 genes). **What measurement shows** (mRNA cistron counts vs `wildtype/basal`, existing local simOut, no new sims): ① `KO:flgB` → all nine of flgBCDEFGHIJ at **0.0 vs WT 5.8**, while flgA/flgK/fliC on other TUs are untouched (it is the TU, not the regulon); `KO:dapA` → dapA **and bamC** both 0.0; `KO:glmS`→glmU, `KO:selA`→selB, `KO:ymgD`→ymgG. ② **the named gene is NOT knocked out** — `KO:rpoB` measured **10.4 vs WT 8.4, no reduction at all**; same class: dnaN, murA, pheS, rpmJ. ③ **silences a gene it is not named after** — `KO:rpmJ` leaves rpmJ at 50.1 (WT 69.5) and takes **secY to 0.0** (WT 15.8). **The asymmetry that survives as usable guidance:** `n_tu==1 → silenced` has **no counterexample in 41** (safe sufficient condition); `n_tu>1 → survives` is **false** (bamC). Co-membership means AT RISK; only measurement settles it. **PROTEIN-LEVEL CONFIRMATION (2026-07-26), answering the 'leftover RNA / wrong species' challenge:** the mRNA verdict was re-tested on MONOMER counts. `KO:flgB` → flgB and flgC protein both **exactly 0.0** (WT ~3,640 and ~3,626), so a genuinely silenced TU leaves *no* residual protein — the leftover-RNA confound is ruled out by construction. `KO:rpoB` → rpoB protein **3,575 vs WT 4,226 (85%)**, rpoC 3,440 vs 4,088 (84%) — and the decisive control, **rpoA, which is NOT on the zeroed TU and is not targeted at all, sits at 7,876 vs 9,710 (81%)**. A gene the knockout cannot touch is reduced *as much as* the gene it supposedly removes, so the ~15% is a global proteome effect (mild growth difference), not rpoB-specific silencing. **The 'rpoB is not knocked out' verdict therefore holds at both mRNA and protein level, across the complex.** ⏳ NOT checked: the per-generation trajectory (`read_species` errors on the KO run roots) and the assembled RNAP holoenzyme count — so a slow multi-generation depletion is not formally excluded, though the rpoA control makes an rpoB-specific one unlikely. **Two project explanations refuted:** "rpoB/dnaN survive on a large inherited RNAP/clamp reserve" (neither was knocked out), and the aaRS gen-3 story must **drop pheS** (still stands on argS/alaS/gltX/lysS, all clean). `KO:flgB` as the "no phenotype BY CONSTRUCTION" control is a nine-gene operon deletion. ✅ **Shipped:** `data/cache/ko_footprint.json` now separates `measured_silenced` / `measured_expressed` / `unverified` with `target_evidence`, plus `data/cache/ko_measured.json`; warnings say MEASURED vs unverified and never upgrade a prediction to a claim; 10 tests pin the measured counts and the bamC counterexample. ⏳ **Owed:** re-word affected prose in `scope.py`/`generate.py`; relabel the five non-KO designs (they cannot be re-run as true single-gene KOs — no monocistronic handle exists); **provenance gap** — nothing records the kb hash or the operon option in the manifest, so operons-ON is filesystem inference, not recorded provenance; and an **rRNA escape hatch** exists (`synth_prob_from_ppgpp(balanced_rRNA_prob=True)` restores a zeroed rRNA TU) — affects ~33 genes, none currently shipped. | **now — publication-blocking** | Two wrong rules caught by insisting on measurement |
| ~~**WELL-6h**~~ ✅ | **✅ Standardized KO verification (`src/cellarium/ko_verify.py`) — the method, not just the answer.** Three verdicts in this thread were wrong from reasoning instead of measuring, and the last was wrong subtly: **a knocked-out cell grows differently, so its WHOLE proteome shifts.** In `KO:rpoB`, rpoB protein reads 85% of wildtype — which looks like a partial knockdown until `rpoA`, which the design cannot touch, reads 81%. **A raw ratio is uninterpretable.** So every verdict is now made against a **null distribution over the genes the design does not target**: "silenced" means far below what happened to everything else. Checked at **mRNA and protein independently** — a genuinely silenced TU reads exactly 0.0 at *both* (flgB/flgC), which is what rules out "silenced but the transcript lingers". **Corpus sweep (14 designs with local raw):** `knocked_out` ×10 (dapA, fabI, flgB, glmS, gltA, lpxC, rpmE, selA, tpiA, ymgD); **`NOT_knocked_out` ×2 — murA (0.968 vs null 0.956), rpoB (0.846 vs null 0.815)**; `partially_reduced` ×1 (rpmJ 0.936 vs null 0.969, and it silences secY); `unmeasurable` ×1 (dnaN). Results cached in `data/cache/ko_verification.json`; 8 CI tests pin the two failure modes on synthetic nulls (a global shift must not read as silencing; and real silencing must survive a *collapsed* proteome — in dapA's run the null median is **0.08**, so a naive "ratio < 0.5" rule would call the entire proteome silenced). ⚠️ **7 designs have NO local raw and are therefore UNVERIFIED**: argS, alaS, gltX, lysS, pfkA, pheS, rplB. | done | The generalisable lesson: never interpret a perturbation ratio without an untargeted control |

| ~~**WELL-6y**~~ | **⚠️ WITHDRAWN — SUPERSEDED by WELL-6z. Right that depth matters, wrong about why, and the remedy was harmful.** This row said "a channel mean is taken over the **whole trajectory**, so depth moves it" and clustered on depth to widen intervals (ICC 0.28–0.85, "ribosome_conc 6.6× too narrow"). A 51-agent adversarial check killed it: the premise is **false**. `_reader_worker.py:203` is `_dynamics(gs[-1])` — a channel is the **LAST generation's** time-mean, not the trajectory's (measured: `growth_rate == per_generation[-1]` on **91/91** rows, `== mean over generations` on **0/91**). So depth does not add variance; it **selects which generation is reported** — gens=1 reports generation 0, gens=7 reports generation 6. The decisive test: at a **matched generation index** the effect vanishes — growth ICC **0.6795 → 0.0000** on the very same lineages. Clustering modelled a deterministic, recorded, fixed effect as random between-group variance, and — worse — widened the interval while leaving the **mean pooled**, so `pct_vs_ref` kept a biased denominator. The quoted "6.6×" was itself a bug artifact (a DEFF-convention mismatch, `n/k` vs Hartley's m₀, over 8 phantom duplicate rows). Struck, not deleted, because it **shipped** in `4c6afa6`. | withdrawn | Second withdrawal on the same observation — the remedy was the error |

Measured to cost nothing — the reference spans all three depths, **zero designs lose their control**, only 4 designs span >1 depth. **This changes real numbers, not just intervals:** against a depth-matched reference, **6 designs flip sign on growth_rate** — the scientifically load-bearing one is **`rRNA_KO:2op`, reported −7.4% → measured +2.2%** (it ran to generation 3 while the pooled reference was generation-0-heavy, so the model's own drift — wild type loses **11% growth over 4 generations in the fitted condition**, balanced-panel measured — was being read as the knockout). **Shipped:** `survey.depth()` names the stratum; `survey_corpus` stratifies the reference and the z-score and discloses `generation_strata` + `designs_spanning_generation_depths` at corpus level (the reference sits at z≈0 *by construction*, so a per-entry note alone is structurally invisible); `differential` flags a **depth mismatch** as a *validity* failure (no widening makes generation 0 comparable to generation 3), naming the shared depth to restrict to; `rigor.disconfirm` compares within a shared depth. **One shared row source (`survey.analysis_rows`) for all three tools** — they had drifted: `disconfirm` reported an interval **5.5× narrower** than `survey_corpus` because it neither filtered `reportable` nor used `design_key` (it keyed on the nullable `condition`), and `differential` averaged in crashed runs. **22 tests** in `tests/test_generation_depth.py`, pinning both withdrawn attempts dead. | done | Third time's the measurement: compare like with like |

Anchored to a path boundary (`(^|/)(runs(_[^/]*)?(/.*)?)$`), verified behaviour-identical on all 273 live paths + 12 adversarial shapes. **(3) The two "invariants" I'd shipped were TAUTOLOGIES** — they asserted only what `QUALIFY` guarantees by construction, so a synthetic wrong-merge passed both. | ~~**WELL-6z2**~~ ✅ | **✅ Four estimator bugs in `stats`, all found while withdrawing 6y, all fixed and pinned.** These are why the withdrawn "6.6×" was never trustworthy even on its own terms. **(1) DEFF used `n/k` after computing Hartley's `m0`** — on sizes [28,4,2] that is 6.60× vs the correct 2.47× (Kish would give 28.04×); a published number chosen by accident. **(2) `df` could exceed `k−1`** — between-cluster variance rests on k−1 df regardless of how many points sit inside; `n_eff−1` claimed df=14 against k−1=2, an interval **2× too narrow on 8/9 channels**, correct on `ribosome_conc` (the quoted one) *only by coincidence*. **(3) SE taken over the undropped list while DEFF dropped singletons** — deleting **1 row of 34 swung the half-width 3.2×**. **(4) the `deff≤1` early return omitted `unreliable`/`df`**, so `info.get("unreliable")` was `None`, neither True nor False. The clustering machinery is **retained** (correct for genuinely exchangeable clusters, which the corpus may yet have) but **no longer applied to depth**. 6 pure tests. | done | The headline number was a convention artifact over phantom rows |

| ~~**WELL-6z6**~~ ✅ | **✅ pgi KO ran (4 seeds × 4 gen, viable, qc=ok) — TWO results.** **(1) A model limitation, confirmed and publishable.** The literature says a `pgi` (phosphoglucose isomerase) KO is *viable but ~50% growth* via forced PPP rerouting + NADPH/redox imbalance (Canonaco 2001; Charusanti 2010). **wcEcoli reproduces the viability but NOT the severity: growth −6.4% vs depth-matched WT** (ppGpp +20%, ribosome +0.3%, no collapse through gen 3). It reroutes with almost no cost — exactly as plain FBA/MOMA predicted (frac 0.98) — so the **whole-cell model inherits the metabolic-FBA redox blind spot**: it does not penalise the NADPH imbalance that cripples real pgi mutants. A concrete, citable divergence for "The Well for the Cell". **(2) The severity-control question is answered anyway — WITHOUT pgi.** pgi failed as a severity control (the model won't make a metabolic KO severe), but the confound WELL-6z3 flagged ("all severe designs are aaRS-lethal, so the aaRS cluster can't be attributed to mechanism") is testable with EXISTING severe-*viable*-non-aaRS designs, and under double-centering (D9/6z4) they cluster by their **own mechanism, not by severity**: `no_oxygen` (−44%) → acetate/succinate/ppGpp (carbon metabolism), **1** weak aaRS neighbour; `rRNA_KO:6op` (−34%) → its own rRNA ladder + ppGpp (ribosome limitation), **0** aaRS; pgi itself → glmS/lpxC/gltA/murA (metabolic), **0** aaRS. So double-centering does NOT force severe designs into one blob — direct evidence it separates severe-mechanism from severe-lethality, strengthening the 6z4 GO. Corpus **264 → 268**, 42 designs. **⚠️ Follow-up (gen-8 run, seeds 4-7, viable to gen 8, no collapse):** the −6.4% is not −50% but not nothing, and 4 generations was no guarantee — so a deeper run tested whether the defect *widens*. It does **not**: the pgi-vs-depth-matched-WT gap opens sharply at gen 1 (−7%) then **PLATEAUS** — −7.4 / −5.9 / −5.9 / −8.4% through gen 4, then noisy near zero (n=4) at gen 5-7, **never trending toward −50% at any depth**. The model settles fast and the redox defect never emerges even given 8 divisions. This **hardens the model-limitation finding**: it is not a matter of needing more generations, the whole-cell model simply reroutes pgi at ~no cost (the FBA redox blind spot). Corpus **268 → 272**. | done | 8 generations confirm it: the model reroutes what biology can't |
| ~~**WELL-6z4**~~ ✅ | **✅ JUSTIFIED — implement DOUBLE-CENTERING, NOT PC1 removal (32-agent audit/test/adversarial/literature, `wfv2tiipg`, + my own reproduction). The concept clears the bar; the literal "subtract PC1" is refuted.** Verified on the live 41-design × 199-species panel: **`corr(growth, cos-to-WT)` +0.608 (baseline) → −0.010 (double-center) vs +0.117 (PC1-removal)**, while the envelope-biosynthesis mechanism cluster {fabI,lpxC,murA,glmS} survives (NN **4/4**, within−overall Δ +0.407 → **+0.358**). **Why not PC1:** PC1 is the GROWTH axis (`corr(PC1,growth)=+0.829`), but the *compositional* distance-from-WT severity WELL-6a actually flagged loads on **PC2** (`corr=+0.908`), so blind PC1 removal leaves compositional severity intact — and PC1's direction wobbles **~27°** across half-splits at n=41 (fitting noise). Double-centering (`Z − rowmean − colmean + grandmean`, parameter-free, no fitted direction, no K) strictly dominates it. **Literature is exact:** the "slow-growth signature ≈ generic stress inflates deletion-strain similarity" is a named phenomenon (O'Duibhir 2014 *Mol Syst Biol*; Klumpp/Hwa 2009 *Cell* growth laws), and the field **warns against blind top-PC subtraction** (Goldinger 2013 *Genetics*; SVA/RUV/ComBat endorse supervised/parameter-free removal) — all citations verified real. **Two mandatory ship-guards:** (a) always surface growth ALONGSIDE the de-confounded similarity (the axis is partly real biology — don't silently erase it); (b) label the **aaRS cluster as severity-confounded** — it stays NN 4/6 under *every* transform and growth-regression *inflates* it (+0.514), so no severity transform can separate aaRS-mechanism from aaRS-lethality. **Residual gaps (do not block the fix):** the aaRS confound needs a *clean non-starvation* severe control — **`pgi` KO is running now** (WELL-6z / Track B) to be that control; and a held-out (seed/contributor) split to validate the transform. **⚠️ Correction to WELL-6z3:** "no severe non-aaRS control exists" is FALSE — `gltX+relA+spoT`, `no_oxygen`, `rRNA_KO:6op` are severe non-aaRS, but all starvation-type; pgi is the clean orthogonal one. Acceptance test: `|corr(growth,cos-to-WT)|<0.15` · envelope cluster Δ>+0.30 · zero hyperparameters. **✅ BUILT — `src/cellarium/similarity.py` + the `similar_designs` tool (analysis-only, Council-blind).** `species_similarity`/`similar_designs`/`acceptance` ship the double-centering + BOTH guards (growth reported alongside; `severity_confounded` flags the collapsing aaRS/dapA/rpmE). Live acceptance: `corr(growth,cos-to-WT)=−0.005` ✓, envelope Δ +0.347 ✓, `passes=True`. **Refinement made while building:** the NN-purity check is now a *diagnostic that names the off-cluster neighbour*, not a gate — because a metabolically-adjacent design legitimately takes an envelope member's NN (pgi became glmS's/lpxC's NN, WELL-6z6), so the gate is the two corpus-robust properties (severity removed + cluster coheres), not a brittle 4/4. 7 tests. The index (WELL-6) still builds late; the metric is done. **Severity-control design assessment (Cellwright + literature, 2026-07-27):** the corpus's most-impaired *viable+stable* designs top out ~−44% (`no_oxygen`) / −34% (`rRNA_KO:6op`); only the aaRS/`dapA`/`rpmE` reach −60%+ and they collapse. Literature archetype for a *viable-but-severe, mechanistically-distinct* single KO: **`pgi`** (phosphoglucose isomerase) — non-essential but "cripples growth" to ~50% via forced PPP rerouting + NADPH/redox imbalance (Canonaco 2001 FEMS; Charusanti 2010 PLoS Genet). **But the cheap predictor can't confirm it:** plain FBA AND MOMA over iML1515 reroute freely (`pgi` frac **0.98**, worst single-KO is `gapA` 0.70) — all "consistent_viable", none severe — so the model's FBA core inherits the redox blind spot the literature documents. **Consequence, two-track:** (Track A) a *known*-severe-viable design (poorer carbon / deeper graded reduction) is a guaranteed control but may overlap existing designs; (Track B — **recommended**) run **`pgi` KO** (variant_index 1984, viable so it won't collapse, ~4 seeds × 4 gen) as an informative gamble: if wcEcoli reproduces the ~50% defect FBA misses → a mechanism-distinct severity control **and** a model-validation win; if it comes out near-WT → a documented whole-cell-model limitation. | **WELL-6d** | **Retrieval at scale is a three-layer stack, in this order** — record so it is not collapsed into "add a vector DB". **(1) Deterministic filter** (needs WELL-1): factor columns → SQL narrows 10⁶ to ~10³; exact, cheap, explainable. **(2) Salience ranking**: today's z-score machinery, unchanged, over the filtered set. **(3) Similarity** (WELL-6b): supplement only. ⚠️ **Methodological constraint, not an engineering preference:** every retrieval step is a *selection*, and a selection made by a model (embedding similarity) is precisely the attention-driven filter that `survey_corpus` was built to replace with arithmetic. Complete, exact, explainable, and it hands back the dose **order**, so a dose-response can be fitted. **(B) Embedding** (nearest neighbour in 10-channel response space): returns `KO:lpxC` (+0.790) **first**, then `ppGpp:0.2x` (+0.781), then `KO:gltA`, `gene_knockout/basal`, `KO:glmS` — **1 of 5 correct**. The point is NOT that the embedding is bad at its job: it correctly reports that lpxC's knockout *came out looking like* a ppGpp perturbation, which is a real and possibly interesting biological observation. It is that **"which runs are experimentally comparable" and "which runs came out similar" are different questions**, and for experimental design only the first one is being asked. Conflating them is the error; using the second to answer the first is 80% noise. | — | Settles the SQL-vs-embedding question with data |
| ~~**WELL-6d2**~~ ✅ | **✅ The four retrieval tools, and the ONE question each answers — recorded here because the answer was previously spread across 6c/6d/6d1 and read as a ranking when it is a partition.** Keeping several is right; must stay primary.** **② Salience ranking (z-score, today)** → *"of those, which MOVED?"* Arithmetic, not attention — the anti-anchoring mechanism. **Ships.** **③ Graph distance over the currency-pruned iML1515** → *"how far apart are these genes MECHANISTICALLY?"* Note this is a **fixed, model-derived object that does not grow with the corpus** — exact all-pairs shortest path over 2,712 nodes is 0.6 s, so it is always computed exactly and never approximated. **Available; keep as an explanatory prior, not as a feature in a predictor** — head-to-head it added ΔMCC +0.010, 95% CI [−0.026, +0.046] over the FBA rule alone (WELL-6c). **④ Response-profile similarity (WELL-6b)** → *"which runs CAME OUT similar?"* The only one that grows with the corpus and the only one that can surprise you — `KO:lpxC` looking like a ppGpp perturbation is a real observation, not a retrieval bug. **Not shippable yet: 3/5, bar is ≥4/5 + rpmE↔rpmJ.** **Graph EMBEDDINGS are the one thing deliberately NOT kept** — on every non-star graph tested the embedding lost to an exact computation over the same object. **The ordering is a methodological invariant, not a preference:** ① and ② must be able to answer alone, because ④ is a *model-made selection* and letting it gate what an agent ever sees reintroduces exactly the attention-driven filter `survey_corpus` exists to replace. ③ and ④ explain and suggest; they must never be the sole path to a design. | ①② now · ③ on demand · ④ at ≥10⁴ runs | Different questions, one ordering — the ordering is the science |
| **WELL-6e** | **The `survey_corpus` context cliff.** `survey_corpus` is an anti-anchoring device *whose mechanism is exhaustiveness* — it hands the agent every design ranked by arithmetic so attention is not what decides. That mechanism has a hard budget: the result must fit in context. It already truncates (now disclosed: 31 of 37 dropped per channel). At ~10⁴ runs "the whole corpus, ranked" **stops existing**, and the tool degrades from "you have seen everything" to "you have seen a sample" — *silently*, because the interface does not change. Owed: a hard rule that any sampled survey reports its sampling fraction, and a decision about what replaces exhaustiveness as the anti-anchoring mechanism. | ~10⁴ runs, or sooner if channels grow | The bias returns through the door built to keep it out |

| **WELL-11** | **MCP surface — DECIDED: sub-agent behind a single tool, local + BYOK.** `ask_cellwright(question)` (and `convene_council`, which needs no corpus at all) runs the full loop over the same `orchestrate` seam the CLI and web app use; the server is spawned by the user's client over **stdio**, so there is no hosting, no auth and no "whose key pays" — the local credential vault already answers that. | **WELL-11a** | **⛔ THE BLINDNESS STAMP IS WITHDRAWN (2026-08-03) — read this first; the rest of the row is kept for the reasoning chain.** What is withdrawn: the `corpus_touched` process flag, the `blindness: blind\|unblinded` field, stamping it into `council_runs`, the hard "a Cellwright-then-Council composite must not ship" prohibition, and the P1-before-ship framing. **Why:** the stamp defended a claim that does not depend on it — the A/B cohort is designated **at creation** (`evals/run_ab.py:167-168`, `:208`, `:323` → `evals/results/ab_ledger.json`) and `evals/aggregate_ab.py:24-36` reads that ledger, **not** `council_runs`, so pre-registration makes the stamping question **moot**. **And the framing itself was wrong:** an *informed* Council — one whose question carries findings from a prior Cellwright investigation — is not contamination, it is the working method of research, and Cellarium should represent the loop rather than police it. The same lesson was already learned once here: a **blocking** Council gate parked ~23/25 canonical questions and was made advisory (M-7). **What replaces it:** a **typed lineage** (`thread_id`/`round_index`/`informed_by`), so blindness is a *query over the chain*, not a stamp on a row — [`docs/DECISIONS.md` **D10**](docs/DECISIONS.md) + [`docs/INVESTIGATION_LOOP.md`](docs/INVESTIGATION_LOOP.md), thread `SP-3`/`SP-3a…f`. The hard prohibition is **converted, not kept**: the composite is legitimate, but must be recorded as **two rounds with an edge**, never one opaque call. **What SURVIVES unchanged:** the three-way call split; the composition analysis (no single tool is wrong); *"a text screen on the question does not fix it"*; provenance-metadata-on-a-legitimate-artifact as the right idea (D10 changes only its data structure); and the additional-calls table. **The real defect the stamp would NOT have fixed:** `M-10`. — *Original assessment follows, unedited:* **⚠️ Surface A (Council-only / Cellwright-only / both + Investigation & Hypothesis readers) — the SPLIT survives; the BLINDNESS ARGUMENT does not.** Full assessment: [`docs/DECISIONS.md` **D6a**](docs/DECISIONS.md). The quarantine we cite is a property of what the **server composes**, not of what the **caller supplies**: `instrument.py:9-12` is import-quarantined and `dial_labels()` is asserted reading-free (`tests/test_blindness.py:153-168`), but `tests/test_blindness.py:19-24` puts `"question"` in `_ALLOWED_KEYS` **unconditionally** and asserts nothing about its contents; `council.deliberate(question: str, ...)` (`council.py:735`) takes free text. In-process a human types it and the only composed path is Council→Cellwright (`orchestrate.py:50-57`). **MCP removes both protections:** the caller is an LLM, and `ask_cellwright` (numbers + run ids) and `read_investigation` (*"the model's full context (every tool input and result)"*, `apps/sessions.py:66-68`) both make it corpus-aware before `convene_council(question=…)` — a legal call. **The leak is by composition; no single tool is wrong.** ⛔ A text screen on the question does NOT fix it (a reading arrives as a paraphrase with no number, no channel, no run id — a string search is not a dependency proof). ✅ What fixes it is **provenance of the question, not inspection of it**: a per-process `corpus_touched` flag set by any corpus-reading call; a `blindness: blind\|unblinded` field on every `convene_council` result; `contains_readings: true` on Investigation/Hypothesis reads; the field stamped into the persisted `council_runs` row (`apps/hypotheses.py:36-38` already has `meta`) so a run cannot be laundered into the record as blind. **It stamps, never blocks** (precedent: M-7's gate is advisory by design). **Hard rule:** a `both` call may exist ONLY server-ordered Council→Cellwright; a Cellwright-then-Council composite must not ship. **Why P1-before-ship:** the A/B claim (`COUNCIL_AB_METHODOLOGY.md`) rests on blindness — without the stamp, `council_runs` becomes a mixture with no column separating it, contaminating the evidence base retroactively. **Additional calls, each with a stated FOR:** `corpus_coverage` → `support.coverage` (`support.py:36`) so a caller can refuse against `MIN_SEEDS=2`/`MIN_GENERATIONS=2` (`support.py:32-33`) before spending a Cellwright loop; `evidence_for(run_ids)` → the evidence ledger (`evidence.py:7`) so a claim in *someone else's* document is traceable without re-running the agent. Not proposed: any launch/write/vault call (D6's shipped default already answers it).  **⚠️ SUPERSEDED IN PART 2026-08-03 — the blindness STAMP is withdrawn; see [`docs/DECISIONS.md` **D10**](docs/DECISIONS.md) and `SP-3`.** The stamp's stated justification was that `council_runs` would become an unseparable mixture and retroactively contaminate the A/B evidence base. It would not: the A/B cohort is **designated at creation** (`evals/run_ab.py:167-168`, `:208`, `:323`) and the powered comparison reads the LEDGER, not the table (`evals/aggregate_ab.py:24-36`). The real defect is `M-10` (substring cohort selection in `scripts/ab_score.py:74` + blindness asserted from `status` at `:86`), which a stamp would not have fixed either. What replaces it: a **typed lineage** (`informed_by`), because an informed Council round is a legitimate artifact — the loop is the method, not a leak. D6a's Cellwright-then-Council prohibition becomes a **representation requirement**: legal, but recorded as two rounds with an edge, never one opaque call. | with WELL-11, before any MCP ship | The invariant is real in-process and assumed over the wire |
| **WELL-11b** | **Surface B — a data-only MCP over the HF corpus (for users who want the DATA without the agents).** Full spec: [`docs/DECISIONS.md` **D6b**](docs/DECISIONS.md). **✅ TURNED INTO A BUILD SPEC 2026-08-03 — [`docs/DECISIONS.md` **D6b-1**](docs/DECISIONS.md), which carries an INVARIANT TABLE (I1–I10) and a TOOL TABLE (T0–T12).** The owner's correction is the reason it exists: arguing *do not expose raw SQL* attacked a strawman — the question was never "SQL tool or nothing", it was **which precise tools to expose over MCP**. So **every invariant raw SQL would let a caller skip came back as a tool that supplies it**: `describe_corpus`, `list_designs`, `check_comparability`, `controls_for`, `get_measurements`, `contrast`, `viability`/`lethality_landscape`, `model_capabilities`, `species`, `raw_access`, `experiment_integrity`, `run_environment` — each with arguments, returns, and **what it refuses and why**; plus **T0**, the deliberately-absent `query(sql)`, whose absence is reported *inside* `describe_corpus` so a caller is told why rather than assuming the surface is immature. **A tool with no invariant does not ship.** The refusal was an architecture, not a do-not-build. Two corrections land there: **(i)** dedup and tombstones are reclassified as *dev-corpus hygiene*, not load-bearing — MEASURED 2026-08-03, the compacted shard is 322 raw → 322 deduped (`DEDUP_QUALIFY` is a no-op) with 0 tombstones, so the surface is designed for a CLEAN corpus of unique runs as the owner scoped; **(ii)** the invariant that *does* survive and is currently unenforced is `kb_sha256` — see **M-11**. Seven honesty requirements, each grounded in how this corpus is actually shaped, not in generic advice. **(B1) Schema discovery cannot be static** — the corpus is a union of per-contributor shards read `union_by_name=true` (`manifest.py:236`, `:449`, `:712`), so columns are partially populated (`manifest.py:874`/`:892` exist to find `kb_sha256 IS NULL` / `elongation_model IS NULL`); report per-column non-null fraction **and which shards supply it**. **(B2) The dedup rule is not the caller's to skip** — `DEDUP_QUALIFY` (`manifest.py:72`) partitions on the PAIR `(id, normalised simout_path)` because neither half is unique (`manifest.py:37-49`); skipping it inflated `wildtype/basal`, the reference for every comparison, from 26 to 34 seeds. **⛔ Therefore NO raw `read_parquet` and NO arbitrary SQL** — a read-only SQL passthrough is the tool that *looks* most honest and is the easiest way to make this corpus lie. **(B3) Tombstones are a THIRD population** (`dropped_keys()`, `manifest.py:88` — excluded from ranking, kept in coverage): every count declares which of deduped-live / +tombstoned / raw rows it counted; WELL-9 (49/60/37) is the standing evidence for what happens otherwise. **(B4) Per-run provenance rides on every row** — `provenance.classify` (`provenance.py:46`); a data-only consumer has no Cellwright system prompt to supply the H1/H2 in-sample caveat (`provenance.py:3-6`). **(B5) Refusal has THREE forms** and conflating them is the failure: not-in-corpus · in-corpus-but-not-readable (`hf.py:23-24` shard-answers vs needs-raw; `_full_simout_local`, `hf.py:27` — a run dir without `simOut/MonomerCounts` is NOT readable; name the recovery route; `raw_available=0` ≠ absent) · below the evidential floor (`support.py:32-33`) which is a refusal at that scope, not a footnote. **(B6) Truncation with named omissions** applies here too — see **PLAT-2**. **(B7) MCP mechanics** (design guidance, not verified against a spec file here): dataset card + per-shard schema report as **resources**; queries as tools with a declared `outputSchema`; `readOnlyHint` on everything (no write path exists); cursor pagination instead of silent truncation; resource links to the backing HF files. **More blocked than Surface A on WELL-1 + WELL-9** — a data-only MCP is nothing but published schema, so it commits the keying scheme completely. | after WELL-1 + WELL-9 + PLAT-2 | The generic "expose read-only SQL" answer is wrong for this corpus |
| **WELL-10** | **codegraph objective 1 (dev-session navigation) is permanently NO-GO** — our *code* is not growing to terabytes, and our coupling lives in string literals, not call edges. Recorded so it is not periodically re-litigated. | never | Closed |

### G3 · Publication-hygiene items the literature says are table stakes

| ID | Item | Literature verdict |
|----|------|--------------------|
| **DATA-SCHEMA** | A **datasheet** for the HF corpus (motivation, composition, collection, preprocessing, uses, distribution). | **Already solved — use the template.** Gebru et al., *Datasheets for Datasets*, arXiv:1803.09010 / CACM 64(12), doi:10.1145/3458723. It is a fill-in question list, so this is **cheaper than an "M"**. Ties to **PUB-A5**: the Composition section *mandates* instance counts, which forces the numbers to match the data. |
| ~~**DATA-PROV**~~ ✅ | **Machine-readable run provenance** rather than a bespoke three-column fix. | **Already solved.** Sandve et al., *Ten Simple Rules for Reproducible Computational Research*, PLOS CB 9(10):e1003285 — Rule 1 is verbatim "whenever a result may be of potential interest, keep track of how it was produced". Use a standard provenance shape, not our own. Overlaps **EVID-1** below. |
| **UX-ROOT-1** | Remove hard-coded relative paths (`data/manifest/*.parquet` resolves against CWD, not the package). | **Standard practice we lack.** Taschuk & Wilson, *Ten simple rules for making research software more robust*, PLOS CB 2017, PMID 28407023 — **Rule 8 is literally "Eliminate hard-coded paths"**. Resolve at startup, not lazily. |
| ~~**UX-TRUST-2**~~ ✅ | Derive the trust strip server-side (single source of truth) **and change what the chip says**. | **Build it, but render the verdict, not a checkmark.** A green "Disconfirmation ✓" is the textbook **automation-bias** pattern; show *what falsifier ran and what it found*. |
| ~~**UX-SEED-3**~~ ✅ | Persist the Council's falsifiers + predicted directions **before** the Cellwright run, so the handoff is an exhibit. | **Standard practice we lack.** Nosek et al., *The preregistration revolution*, PNAS 2018, PMID 29531091 — the blindness invariant the architecture already implements *is* preregistration; persisting it makes it evidence rather than narration. |

## Deep-Dive Register — ship-then-deepen

Functionality that **ships working** but that we deliberately want to **revisit and harden later** across seven
rigor axes: **`SCI`** scientific validity · **`MTH`** methodology · **`DS`** data science / statistics ·
**`ML`** machine learning · **`AGT`** agentic reasoning · **`TCV`** toolchain validity · **`ENG`** engineering.
This is *not* a bug list — each item works today; the register records the honest next depth so the shipped-fast
version doesn't quietly become the final word. When an item is deepened, strike it and note the outcome.

**Status at a glance — three buckets:**
- ✅ **Done (code shipped, CI-green):** DD-ENG-1, DD-ENG-2, DD-ENG-3, DD-ENG-4, DD-ENG-5, DD-MTH-1, DD-MTH-2, DD-AGT-1, DD-SCI-4a (a/b/c), DD-SCI-5b, DD-TCV-1 (b/c). The engineering-deepening pass from the loop-review audit is complete; DD-ENG-4/5 are the two integrity bugs the adversarial audit surfaced (`reconcile` false-'done', `data_availability` fabricated availability) — both fixed; DD-ENG-6 adds proactive resource-warning tools so Cellwright steers the user away from a sweep that would exhaust their machine.
- ⏳ **Phase-2-gated (blocked on running sim SWEEPS — compute/budget, not code):** DD-SCI-5a (grow the surrogate's labels → calibration / nested-CV), DD-SCI-4a(d) (close the retrain loop), DD-DS-1 (eval harness over corpus snapshots), DD-MTH-3 *run* (choose `COUNCIL_TEMPERATURE` empirically), plus the `temperature_sweep` / `loop_live` eval runs. All need the corpus to GROW, which needs the operational pivot — the code + plumbing are already in place.
- 📌 **Documented decisions (settled — no code owed):** DD-TCV-1(a) and DD-AGT-2 (see their rows). These were live *questions*; both are now ruled.
- 🎯 **Publication-readiness gates (the load-bearing weakness — mostly methodology + Phase-2 runs, NOT code):** PUB-A1…A5 — see the *Publication-readiness* subsection below. This is what actually stands between the artifact and a publishable methods paper.

| ID | Axis | Feature | The deeper dive we owe it |
|----|------|---------|---------------------------|
| **DD-SCI-5a** | ML · DS · SCI | Viability surrogate (`surrogate.py`) | n≈20 is a toy: (a) **grow the labelled set** via Phase-2 sweeps, then re-fit; (b) move beyond LOO to **nested CV** + a held-out test once n allows; (c) **calibration** (reliability curve / Brier), not just accuracy/MCC — a "0.72 probability" must mean 0.72; (d) **richer features** (pathway membership, TU co-regulation, FBA single-KO flux) with regularization tuned to avoid overfitting; (e) honest **uncertainty on each prediction** (conformal / bootstrap interval), not a bare probability; (f) compare against the deterministic scope-rule baseline to prove the ML earns its keep. |
| ~~**DD-SCI-5b**~~ | ✅ | Surrogate **target** — DECIDED + parked | **Target stays the sim's verdict**: the surrogate is an EMULATOR of the sim (compute reduction), not a biology predictor. Rejected on merits: (B) predicting the Baba/Joyce benchmark is redundant + weaker than FBA (whole stoichiometry) and forfeits `essential_ref` as the dominant feature; (C) predicting the sim-vs-benchmark *disagreement* is a category error — `model_UNDER_predicts` is a near-deterministic function `scope`/`model_validation` already compute in closed form (positive class n~3-5), nothing to learn. Recorded in `surrogate.py`. Added a machine-checked **significance flag** (`train` → label-permutation LOO-MCC p-value + Wilson accuracy CI): on the live n=20 corpus it honestly reports **NOT significant** (p=0.11; baseline 0.70 inside the CI [0.53, 0.89]) — the parked state. **PARKED until the corpus is large enough**; the plumbing is ready to resume (this is the honest slice of DD-SCI-5a). **Done** — see Completed. |
| **DD-SCI-4a** | SCI · AGT · MTH · *2 left* | Multi-KO / reduced-genome generator (`design_generator.py`) | ✅ (a) **`fba_synthetic_lethal` folded in** — the FBA pairwise-lethality test (second oracle over iML1515) runs on the candidates the single-gene prior calls VIABLE (where it's blind); a hit demotes propose→flag + surfaces the reroute-blocker. ✅ (b) **active-learning objective** — `information_gain` scores EXPECTED SURPRISE from the two oracles: `oracle_disagreement` (prior says viable × FBA says synthetic-lethal — dominates), `prior_uncertainty` (surrogate near 0.5), `fba_fragility` (double grows but weakly). `generate(objective="information")` ranks the runs that TEACH the most first (a predicted-viable-yet-FBA-lethal reroute-blocker leads, even though `viability` buries it as 'flag'); glass-box `surprise_why`. Verified live + mock-tested. ✅ (c-i) **full k-way deletion** — `fba.fba_gene_deletion` (tool `fba_gene_deletion`) knocks out the WHOLE set at once (1 LP) + `lethal` flag; `deletion_check` folds it in at k≥3, so `_fold_epistasis` demotes a set lethal via a synthetic-lethal PAIR **or** the full k-way deletion (higher-order lethality the pairwise scan misses); `information_gain` reads it. Verified live (`pfkA+tpiA+gapA` → 0.70). ✅ (c-ii) **greedy / beam search** — `generate(search="greedy"|"beam")` builds size-k sets incrementally, keeping the top-`beam_width` PARTIAL sets per step (`_beam_search`, greedy = width 1), so it scores **O(width·k·|pool|)** sets not `C(|pool|,k)` — what makes the FBA-heavy scoring tractable at large pools/k (n=20,k=5: greedy 90 / beam-10 540 evals vs 15,504 full). Validated against brute-force enumeration (a wide beam == exhaustive optimum; a planted trap shows beam recovers what greedy misses). ⏳ (d) close the corpus→surrogate→generator→sim→corpus retrain loop *(Phase-2-gated)*. |
| ~~**DD-TCV-1**~~ ✅ | TCV · AGT · ENG · *1 left* | Council ↔ tool-vocabulary coupling | Reverse-exhaustiveness invariant classifies every tool (nameable-test vs analysis-only) — done. ✅ (c) the proposer's decision-rule EXAMPLES + 'not available' note are now **generated from the registry** (`test_registry.proposer_guidance()` — each supported spec's new `example` + each unsupported spec's `caveat`) and embedded in `_PROPOSER_SYS`, so the prompt can't drift from the enum; **fixed the live drift** it exposed (the prompt kept coaching the flat `\|t\|>=2` after DD-MTH-1 made `disconfirm` df-aware — the `welch_disconfirm` doc + example are now `p<0.05` at the Welch df). ✅ (b) CI guards (`test_harness`/`test_council`): the guidance surfaces every supported example + every unsupported caveat, the prompt embeds it, and the stale `\|t\|>=2` is gone. 📌 (a) **DECIDED — no.** A grounded verification test (the M-8 `robustness_check` panel) is **never Council-nameable**, and the reverse-classification invariant already enforces it (`ANALYSIS_ONLY_TOOLS`). *The question:* the Council names a statistical test in each falsifier — should the robustness panel be in that vocabulary? *The ruling:* no, on two independent grounds. (1) **Blindness:** the panel operates ON the per-seed grounded evidence, which the blind Council never sees — it *cannot* run it, so it must not name it; the falsifier is Cellwright's to execute + verify post-hoc. (2) **Semantics:** the panel is a *meta*-verification of a conclusion, not a *primary discriminating* test — putting it in the falsifier `test_id` enum would invite the Council to name it *instead of* a real discriminator (welch/slope/BC). If we ever want the Council to *recommend* a confirmation step, that is a separate, deliberate addition — not a default. **No code owed.** |
| ~~**DD-AGT-1**~~ ✅ | AGT · MTH | Loop architecture (verdict, 2026-07-20 review `wf_0746718c`) | 20-agent literature-review + adversarial audit. **Verdict: the loop family is already right** — Cellwright is a bounded, grounded **ReAct** loop; rigor.py + the M-8 panel are the **CRITIC / LLM-Modulo external verifier**; the Council is a **blind prover-verifier debate** (judge = gate, scalable-oversight-as-control); compaction is **MemGPT-style**; the whole is a *closed data loop inside an open, human-driven science loop*, deliberately severed by the blindness invariant + the approval airlock. **Do not** add autonomous/tree-search/retry loops — the audit rejected them (Goodhart, provenance-laundering, optional-stopping, control confounds). Remaining work is deterministic hardening (below), not a new architecture. |
| ~~**DD-ENG-1**~~ | ✅ | Tool-call error isolation *(shipped)* | `tools.dispatch` now catches EVERY tool-body exception (was only `TypeError`) and returns a structured `{"error", "error_kind"}` the model can read/retry/report — so a KeyError/ValueError/DuckDB/IO/cobra/pydeseq2 failure no longer unwinds the whole `converse()` turn and discards the answer. `error_kind` tags `internal_defect` (logged ERROR + full traceback, so a real bug is never hidden) vs `runtime`/`arguments` (logged terse). dispatch is now total, so the `agent.py:441` call site is inherently safe. Host-tested (`test_analysis_tools.py`: runtime/defect/type paths + the never-raises contract). **Done** — see Completed. |
| ~~**DD-MTH-1**~~ | ✅ | Deterministic power grounding in M-8 + df-aware significance *(shipped)* | (a) `robustness_check` now runs a **fixed, always-run, escalation-blind** deterministic power floor: `tools.power_check` (corpus replicate CV → MDE) rules the effect **underpowered** when it's below the noise floor, overriding the panel — so 'underpowered' is an MDE ruling, not a juror's feel (and never verdict-triggered → avoids the optional-stopping trap). (b) `rigor.disconfirm` now uses `stats.welch_t` (Welch–Satterthwaite **df-aware** p<0.05) instead of the flat `\|welch_t\|>=2.0`, which was wrong at small df (t-crit at df=2 ≈ 4.3) and contradicted the t-CIs; surfaces `welch_df`/`welch_p`. Host-tested (`test_robustness.py`: the underpowered override + adequately-powered path). **Done** — see Completed. |
| ~~**DD-MTH-2**~~ | ✅ | Reproducibility of the Council control — pinned, at its OWN value *(not Cellwright's 0.0)* | The Council now pins its own **warm** temperature BY CONSTRUCTION (`council.COUNCIL_TEMPERATURE`, default 1.0 = the eval's existing regime, now fixed so a run reproduces; resolved inside `deliberate` so the eval A/B, tests, and any bare call all get it — an explicit float still wins for the sweep). It is **asymmetric to Cellwright's 0.0 BY PURPOSE** — the Council is a blind hypothesis *generator* (exploration is its function), Cellwright a faithful *analyst* — so it is NOT routed through `agent.temperature_for`. Fixed the latent bug where the live path ran the Council at 0.0 (via `temperature_for`) while the eval ran it unpinned at ~1.0 — now unified. **Reasoning-vs-non-reasoning note (recorded):** temperature is a lever ONLY on non-reasoning models; on opus / with extended thinking the API forces temperature=1 and rejects an explicit value, so `_council_temperature` returns `None` — a Council on opus is exploratory-by-force but non-reproducible, one on sonnet is pinnable. Host-tested (`test_council.py`). **Done** — see Completed. |

Pure metric layer host-tested. ⏳ **Run** it (API budget) to choose `COUNCIL_TEMPERATURE` empirically — the current 1.0 is a placeholder. See the Evaluations section. |

(2b) `_provenance_ledger` extracts a compact `tool(args) → {numbers + run_ids/provenance}` line from every old tool call BEFORE `_summarize` drops the raw results, and `compact_history` preserves it verbatim (both the LLM-summary and no-LLM fallback paths) — so a number the agent cites after compaction still traces to its tool + run, not just the lossy Haiku summary. Host-tested (`test_agent.py`). **Done** — see Completed. |
| ~~**DD-ENG-3**~~ | ✅ | Loop operational hygiene *(shipped)* | `run`'s stale `max_turns=8` now inherits `converse`'s 12 (converse is the single source); the "~25 tools" doc drift is fixed to a **dynamic** `len(tools.TOOLS)` (never drifts again); and a **circuit breaker** stops the loop when an identical `(tool, args)` call repeats `_REPEAT_CAP=3×` or `_ERR_CAP=3` consecutive all-error rounds occur — a stuck agent falls through to the forced synthesis instead of burning the whole budget. Host-tested (`test_agent.py`). **Done** — see Completed. |
| **DD-DS-1** | DS · MTH | Surrogate + generator **evaluation harness** | Neither has a standing eval that tracks quality *as the corpus grows*. Owe: a small `evals/` harness that re-fits the surrogate and re-scores the generator on each corpus snapshot and logs calibration/MCC/coverage over time (so "it sharpens as data grows" is measured, not asserted). |
| **DD-AGT-2** | AGT · MTH · *📌 decided + ⏳ Phase-2 run* | Heterogeneous Council judge — an **ablation**, not a default | *The question:* should the Council's JUDGE use a different-family base model (`CELLARIUM_JUDGE_MODEL`) to guard against a single-family shared blind spot (cf. the aaRS-KO band-aid at council.py)? 📌 **DECIDED — not by default.** The Council is the paper's designated CONTROL; making the judge a distinct family injects a **model-family confound** — convergence could no longer be attributed to the debate STRUCTURE vs inter-model prior differences, undermining every A/B. So the DEFAULT stays same-family (the knob already exists, `_default_models`, and is left non-default). ⏳ **Phase-2 run (not code):** execute it as a *reported ablation* on the canonical questions and measure prover/verifier error-correlation — this needs API budget, so it's queued with the other Phase-2 eval runs, not owed as an implementation. |
| ~~**DD-ENG-4**~~ | ✅ ENG · *integrity, adversarial audit `wf_57388055`* | `reconcile` false-'done' | A multi-seed campaign that crashed after seed 0 was reported **`done`** — `launch.reconcile` used `manifest.has_run` (label-prefix match that stops before `s{seed}`), which is true if **≥1** seed landed. A partial run + null shard hid behind a green status in a rigor-first tool. **Fixed:** new `manifest.count_runs` (DISTINCT seed-labels); `reconcile` compares it to the requested seed count → `done` only when all landed, else **`partial`** (with a `k/N seeds` error), else `failed`. Residual: labels are seed- but not campaign-scoped, so a design *re-run* across campaigns still pools seeds (noted). Host-tested (`test_launch.py`). **Done.** |
| ~~**DD-ENG-6**~~ | ✅ ENG · *responsible stewardship* | Proactive resource-warning tools | `resources.py` + two agent tools Cellwright can SEE — `system_resources` (live free RAM/disk/CPU + Docker status + corpus footprint) and `estimate_sim_resources` (a proposed sweep's RAM/disk COST vs what's free → verdict `ok`/`warn`/`block` + a safe `recommended_parallel`/`recommended_chunk_runs`, grounded in the corpus's real ~GB/run). The Cellwright system prompt now instructs it to check these BEFORE queuing a multi-run sweep and WARN the user in plain terms + offer the chunked plan, instead of filling the disk or thrashing RAM. No hard dep (psutil if present, else ctypes/proc; graceful when RAM unreadable). Classified `ANALYSIS_ONLY` (invariant holds); host-tested (`test_resources.py`). **Hardened per a 6-agent SOTA lit-review (`wf_ed2b02a4`)** which caught a real correctness bug + added the cheap high-value signals: (i) **Docker-VM ceiling** — sized RAM against host-free but the sim runs INSIDE the WSL2/HyperV VM (capped ~50% of host), so it would over-schedule into a silent OOM; now sizes against `min(host_free, docker-info MemTotal − overhead)` + VM CPU (`effective_ram_free_gb`); (ii) **tightest disk volume** — checks the system/temp drive (where the Docker vhdx lives) not just the output drive; (iii) **wall-clock budget** — warns on a multi-hour sweep → chunk+queue. ⏳ Remaining lit-review must-adds *(follow-ups, small/medium)*: inode budget + `--shm-size`; per-chunk **re-admission** (re-check between chunks); **I/O-ceiling** cap on parallel (the SSD-vs-HDD "parallel-6 wall"); **OOMKilled/exit-137** adaptive backoff; a SEPARATE token/$ governor for the API-bound eval track (do NOT fold into RAM). Deliberately NOT building datacenter machinery (PSI daemons, graded ladders, cgroup tuning). **Done (core + the bug fix).** |
| ~~**DD-ENG-5**~~ | ✅ ENG · *integrity, adversarial audit* | `data_availability` fabricated availability | It reported `available: true` + a copy-paste `hf download` command from the `HF_HAS_RAW` flag + a string transform, **without checking HF** — so it advertised downloads for runs not on the Hub (while `download_plan`/`download_raw` *do* the real check). An honesty gap in a project whose pitch is a shippable honest dataset. **Fixed:** `data_availability` now does the same `_repo_sizes` (HfApi) existence check — `available`/command only when the archive is **confirmed present**; `verified:false` + a "could not verify (offline)" or "NOT on HF" status otherwise. Host-tested (`test_hf.py`). **Done.** |

  *what depth remains* — the register is a rigor ledger, not a TODO dump.

### Publication-readiness — from the adversarial audit (`wf_57388055`, 2026-07-21)

An independent 6-lens adversarial critique (verified against the code) found the load-bearing weakness is **not code —
it's the eval**, which as framed cannot support one inferentially valid quantitative claim. These are the gates to a
publishable methods paper; most are **methodology + Phase-2 runs**, not implementation. Recorded so they're not lost.

| ID | Gate | The problem + the fix |
|----|------|-----------------------|
| **PUB-A1** | Replicate the headline A/B | `run_ab.py` runs each case **n=1** per arm, no error bars, arms on non-comparable metrics, pass/fail from a single judge that flips run-to-run. **Plan drafted → `docs/PUB_A1_REPLICATION.md`** (k reps/case, a shared graded endpoint for both arms, case-clustered mean±CI + a paired test, a pre-registered discriminating secondary since the primary rubric saturates). **Key clarification: this is API-bound, NOT memory-bound** — it reads the existing corpus + makes model calls, launches no sims — so the blocker is `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (unset here), not RAM/disk. ✅ **All offline pieces shipped.** `run_ab.py --reps N` (+`--out`) writes N replicate cells per case (keys `cid#r{rep}`); `evals/aggregate_ab.py` turns them into per-cell mean±CI + a **case-clustered paired** comparison. **`evals/shared_metric.py` closes the last gap — the arms were never scored on the same thing:** Arm B was graded against the case rubric, Arm A got only `corpus_reads`/`answer_chars`/tool-error counts, so the headline comparison had **no shared quality axis at all**. Both arms now emit **`quality_score`** (fraction of `min+stringent` criteria passed) from one judge on one rubric, **blind to the arm** — the payload is a bare `candidate_answer` plus the rubric, no arm label and no structured `Hypothesis` JSON, because Arm B emits structure and Arm A emits prose and a judge that could tell them apart would reward FORMAT over science (asserted purely, no API call). Prompt says "candidate answer", never "hypothesis". `grade.deterministic()` stays Arm-B-only on purpose — it inspects a `Hypothesis` object Arm A never produces. ⚠️ **Framing confound, documented:** the arms get different task framings and the rubric is hypothesis-shaped, so an unmatched run partly measures framing; `--matched-framing` gives Arm A the same artifact-shape instruction (without leaking the rubric or answer key) and is the comparison to quote. **Run both and report the gap — it IS the confound's size.** Also fixed while exercising the chain: `_paired_t`'s `or 1e-12` epsilon would have reported **p≈0 from a degenerate zero-variance sample** — reachable, since `quality_score` is a fraction over ~3-6 criteria — now an exact **sign test** with an honest floor (2·0.5ⁿ). ⏳ Owed: the live run (needs the key; API-bound, not memory-bound) + the PUB-A2 cross-family panel (`shared_metric.grade()` takes client+model, so the blind payload is already panel-ready). |
| **PUB-A2** | Fix the grader (it's noisier + more self-preferential than what it grades) | `grade.py` uses a single-sample, temperature-1 (opus forces it), no-inter-rater-reliability, **same-family** Opus judge that also **sees the answer key**. Owe: a **pre-registered cross-family judge panel + human validation** on a subset; the one significant ablation cell rides a degenerate temp-0 GPT-4o grader and *flips* under `generic_judge`. ✅ **Prerequisite shipped 2026-07-26 — the panel is now cheap instead of a re-run.** `shared_metric.grade()` returns **`graded_text`** (the exact string judged) and **`judge_model`**, so the ledger is self-contained: a panel is a loop over stored artifacts, not a re-run of the sweep. This is load-bearing twice over — an inter-rater statistic is only meaningful if every rater scores the **same items**, which is impossible if re-grading means re-rolling each arm. ⚠️ **Also fixed, and it would have silently cost the sweep:** every Arm A replicate wrote the **same session id** (`s_eval_<case>`), so `SessionStore.put` overwrote all but the last — a `--reps 5` run would have kept 5 scores and **1 transcript**, discoverable only after paying for it. Both pinned by tests. ✅ **The panel itself shipped 2026-07-26 — `evals/panel.py`, offline half complete, 14 tests, no key needed to verify it.** `--dry-run` prices the run and refuses to hide anything; `--panel-out` makes it resumable. It reports **three numbers, deliberately not collapsed into one**: **① Krippendorff's α** between judges (interval-scaled, since `quality_score` is a fraction — nominal would call 0.4-vs-0.6 exactly as wrong as 0.0-vs-1.0), **② within-judge SD** from repeated sampling of a fixed artifact — the grader's own noise, which no number of replicates can fix — and **③ decision stability**: the headline paired test re-run *once per judge*, shouting if the sign or significance disagrees. Plus **effect-to-noise**, the comparison that decides whether the sweep is readable at all. α is pinned against **Krippendorff's own published worked example** (4 observers × 12 units with missing cells → **0.7434 vs the published 0.743**) rather than against a reimplementation, which would only prove two copies of the same formula agree. Deliberate edge-case choices, each tested: a constant rating returns α **undefined, not 1.0** (a judge that always says 0.5 is not a flawless judge); negative α is **not clamped** (worse-than-chance and chance say different things about the rubric); single-rater units are dropped *and reported*. ⚠️ **What the dry run already tells us: the existing n=1 ledger — all 50 artifacts — CANNOT be panel-validated.** It predates `graded_text`, so validating the published HARK A/B result would mean re-running it. The panel is usable from the *next* sweep onward. **Still owed:** the ≥2-family judge list (a single-family panel measures noise but not self-preference, and `panel.py` says so out loud), and the ~25-artifact human-graded anchor. |
| **PUB-A3** | The primary ablation metric is a saturated null | The pre-registered operationalization-quality rubric scores ~6/6 for every config incl. single-shot `proposer_only` (Wilcoxon p=1.0) — the multi-agent Council shows **no gain on its own designed endpoint**. ~~Owe: retire/reframe the tautological metric; move to a discriminating one.~~ **⚠️ RE-SCOPED 2026-07-28 — the stated fix was ALREADY DONE; the real blocker is PUB-A2.** Verified on the stored results, no new spend: **(1) The saturation is exact, not approximate.** The full-vs-`proposer_only` paired difference has **SD = 0.000** across all 120 records — the rubric is a *constant*, so it cannot discriminate at ANY n (not underpowered: mathematically incapable). Cause: the six criteria map 1:1 onto `Hypothesis` schema fields, so the metric scores **schema compliance**, which any competent proposer achieves every time. **(2) A replacement was already pre-registered AND run** — `PREREGISTRATION.md` §1b added the **residual-defect count** (blind adversarial auditor, `evals/audit.py`), and `evals/results/audited.json` holds 120 audited records. It *does* vary and favours the Council: `full` 3.37 vs `proposer_only` 4.50 defects (Claude judge), **−0.967 with 95% CI [−1.312, −0.621] excluding zero** (GPT judge). **(3) But the two judges are UNCORRELATED: r = −0.097 (n=120)** — and they disagree on the winner (Claude ranks `full` best, GPT ranks `generic_judge` best, which would mean the falsifiability rubric adds nothing). So the endpoint separates configs while the raters disagree at chance. **Consequence: more cases cannot fix this** — validity is bounded by reliability (the same ordering caveat already recorded for PUB-A1/A2; Thakur et al. arXiv:2406.12624: no LLM-judge endpoint is publishable without an agreement statistic). **PUB-A3 is therefore blocked on PUB-A2's judge panel, not on a missing metric.** Exploratory offline probes of further endpoints (specificity/executability/binding) are recorded in the session but all had CIs spanning zero at n=10 and are NOT proposed as replacements. ⏸️ **Interpretation deliberately left open — the Council/operationalization workstream is Filippo's (D1–D6); the metric decision needs that conversation, not a unilateral call.** 15 of 25 cases remain held out for a confirmatory run once the judge is trustworthy. |
| **PUB-A4** | "Blind" ≠ blind to the *answer* | The quarantine seals the corpus *file*, but the Council runs on a frontier LLM that holds the textbook result to ~23/25 cases in-weights, while the grader asserts the generator "did NOT" see it. Owe: **timestamp-lock the rubric** + scope the claim to the genuinely-unknowable out-of-sample cases (argS etc.). |
| ~~**PUB-A5**~~ ✅ | Scale the "The Well for the Cell" claim to what ships | The pitch outruns the artifact (~265-run manifest, ~13-design raw subset). Owe: lead with the **induction-vs-repression scope map** as the modest defensible core, and size the dataset claim to the ~5 MB manifest + the raw subset that actually uploads. |

## Evaluations — the paper's measurement layer (`evals/`)

Every eval script lives in `evals/` (spec: `evals/EVAL_SPEC.md`, cases: `evals/cases.py`, outputs:
`evals/results/`). Recorded here so the measurement layer is catalogued in one place. Status: **✅ run** = has
produced results in `evals/results/`; **⚙ ready** = script works + tested, awaiting a live run (API budget).

| Script | What it measures | Status |
|--------|------------------|--------|
| `run_ab.py` | The **Council-vs-Cellwright A/B** at scale — Arm A (grounded Cellwright, sighted) vs Arm B (blind Council), persisted to the app's `sessions.db`. The paper's headline comparison. | ⚙ **ready — the live sweep is the last open PUB-A1 step (see TODO below)**; the existing result is *n=1* ([[hark-ab-result]] — a methodological pre-registration, not a sweep) |
| `shared_metric.py` | **PUB-A1: the one rubric both arms are scored on** — `quality_score`, arm-blind, format-neutral. Data + judge call; no runner. Retains `graded_text`, which is what makes `panel.py` a re-grade rather than a re-run. | ⚙ ready *(unit-tested pure; awaiting the live sweep)* |
| `panel.py` | **PUB-A2: the cross-family judge panel** — re-grades the *stored* artifacts with N judges × M samples and reports Krippendorff's α, within-judge noise, and whether the headline conclusion **survives a change of judge**. `--dry-run` prices it for free. | ⚙ ready *(α pinned to Krippendorff's published 0.743; needs a ≥2-family judge list + human anchor)* |
| `aggregate_ab.py` | Replicated-ledger roll-up: per-cell mean±CI + the **case-clustered paired** arm comparison (sign-test fallback on zero variance). | ⚙ ready *(unit-tested on synthetic rows)* |
| `ablate.py` | **Role/gate ablation + baselines** (full / no_skeptic / proposer_only / generic_judge / leaked) × cases × reps, graded on the 6-criterion operationalization rubric (Claude + cross-family GPT judge). | ✅ run |
| `aggregate.py` | Rolls up the ablation records → per-config operationalization-quality (mean ± 95% CI), convergence, feasibility. | ✅ run |
| `audit.py` | **Adversarial residual-defect audit** of ablation hypotheses — the sensitive mechanism metric once the binary rubric saturates. | ✅ run |
| `debate.py` | **Operationalization debate** — Council vs Cellarium agent as opposing counsel, blind cross-examination with self-verdicts. | ✅ run |
| `grade.py` | Per-case deliberate + grade against the literature rubric (the two-layer grader). | ✅ run |
| `human_packet.py` | Builds a **blinded human-evaluation packet** (two hypotheses randomized: Council vs single-shot) for judge-validity (Workstream D2). | ⚙ ready |
| `loop_live.py` | **Closed-loop verdict engine** — turns a Council `Hypothesis` into an EXECUTED test on fresh sim output → confirm/refute (Workstream A1/D1). | ⚙ ready *(needs sim output)* |
| `cases.py` | The machine-readable **case corpus** + literature-derived rubrics (data, not a runner). | — |
| **`temperature_sweep.py`** *(DD-MTH-3, new)* | **Council temperature sweep** — canonical questions × a temperature grid × replicates, on a non-reasoning model, reporting per-temperature operationalization quality, convergence, and cross-replicate **diversity** (reuses `ablate.py`'s grading). Chooses `COUNCIL_TEMPERATURE` empirically (currently a placeholder 1.0). Pure metric layer host-tested (`test_temperature_sweep.py`). | ⚙ ready — **run it** to set the value |

### ⏳ TODO — the live PUB-A1 sweep (the last step that needs a key)

**The only remaining PUB-A1 work.** Everything offline is built, tested and green; this is a billable, hours-long
run on Evangelos's machine. It is **API-bound, not memory-bound** — it reads the existing corpus and makes model
calls, and launches **no** simulations, so Docker is not needed and the resource preflight does not apply.

Run it **twice** — with and without `--matched-framing` — because the gap between them *is* the size of the
task-framing confound, and reporting either number without naming the mode is the easiest way to overclaim:

```bash
# clean comparison (both arms asked for the same artifact shape)
python evals/run_ab.py 1.1 4.1 4.2 --reps 5 --matched-framing --out evals/results/ab_matched.json
python evals/run_ab.py 5.2 5.3 5.5 --reps 5 --matched-framing --out evals/results/ab_matched.json   # append
# ...batches of ~3 cases; resumable — a rate-limit or a sleeping laptop never loses work
python evals/aggregate_ab.py evals/results/ab_matched.json --metric quality_score

# framing-confounded baseline (Arm A answers the question as a user would ask it)
python evals/run_ab.py <same batches> --reps 5 --out evals/results/ab_baseline.json
```

**Design to pre-register before spending anything:** k=5 reps (k=8–10 if budget allows) × ~14 canonical cases ×
2 arms ≈ 140 arm-runs, order 2–4k model calls. **Checklist:** ① timestamp-lock the rubric (commit+tag `cases.py`
*before* the first run — PUB-A4); ② decide the case list up front and do not revise it after seeing results;
③ record `reps_per_cell` + `n_paired_cases` as the design in the paper; ④ report effect size + CI, never p alone;
⑤ scope the claim to the genuinely out-of-sample cases (argS etc.), not the ~23/25 the model knows in-weights.
**Do not** fold `grade.deterministic()` into the comparison — it is Arm-B-only by construction.
Blocked-on afterwards: PUB-A2's cross-family judge panel + IRR (`shared_metric.grade()` already takes
client+model, and its payload is arm-blind, so a panel is a loop over judges plus an agreement statistic).

The pfk design and its parameters were otherwise **unrecorded** — its raw was pruned locally on 2026-07-12 after HF upload.
  - **The three `multi_gene_knockout_*` directories are not three runs**: one complete run, one HF-only (pruned, recoverable), and one that is the model's transit dir (`multi_gene_knockout_000000`, evacuated by `runner.run_one`).
  - **CODE-vs-KB ATTRIBUTION (the decisive experiment, enabled by EXT-PORT-13 below).** Native code on the **fork's** `sim_data` reproduces the fork **bitwise**: gltX+relA+spoT **2529/2529 timesteps identical** on cellMass and ppGpp; wildtype **212 of 212 comparable biological columns identical** (the only 17 that differ are `EvaluationTime/*` wall-clock instrumentation, plus its width 18→19 for the extra listener; **32 variable-length columns were NOT compared** — `raw.read_column` cannot parse them); pfkA+pfkB **0.000 % on all 7 manifest channels**. **100 % of the observed difference is the refitted ParCa**, which shifts ppGpp by **−28.08 %** on the dead-cell (gltX) trajectory while cellMass never moves more than **0.925 %**.
  - **The ppGpp drift is systematic, not chaotic** — monotone (−0.5 → −30.5 %) with the mass trajectories locked together, unlike the wildtype's sign-flipping (−15 → −1 → +10 → +25 %) which only opens after the masses separate at step 1395. **Open: why the refit moves ppGpp 28 % — and whether ParCa is deterministic.**
  - **EXT-PORT-13 · the listener gate** (`model_overlay/files/models/ecoli/listeners/trna_charging.py`). `TrnaCharging` is registered unconditionally and read `sim_data.relation.codons` in `initialize()`, so a **steady-state** run — which writes none of the columns those attributes size — could not START against a pre-port `sim_data`. The gate it needed (`self._kinetic_path`) was already computed **8 lines above the read**. Now: required (still raising) on the kinetic path, defaulted on the steady-state path. Negative control: same command, same kb, **without** the fix → `AttributeError`, variant FAILED, **exit code 0 both times**. Pinned by `tests/test_trna_listener_gate.py` (6 tests; **4 fail on the reverted code**). `scripts/build_model_overlay.py:carry_patches()` stops the next rebuild from silently reverting it — `write_overlay` rmtree's `model_overlay/files` and re-harvests from `--source`.

The two blocks in `runner.run_one` are now in the correct order (`_reached` moved with it, for the same reason). Pinned by `tests/test_item7_defects.py::test_calibration_is_observed_after_the_multi_ko_move`, which reads the source order and **fails on the reverted ordering (verified)**. The polluted observations from this session were discarded, not committed.
  - **The kb difference is ONE ROW, proven by reversion** — `EG11283_RNA` (phnE1) typed `mRNA`→`pseudo` in `rnas.tsv` (EXT-PORT-10). Reverting that single row and refitting restores the fork's `exp_ppgpp` **bit-identically (0/3276 differ, max 0.00%)**; `gatZABC[c]` and `TU874[c]` return from **exactly zero** to 3.514768e-05 / 2.351765e-06, cistrons 4538→4539. Mechanism is the degenerate global `fast_nnls` at `transcription.py:728` already documented in `docs/DECISIONS.md`; this measures its **phenotype** cost for the first time. **phnE1 STAYS `pseudo`** — see the decision record. Our port's `fit_sim_data_1.py` diff vs upstream contains **ZERO ppGpp-touching lines**, and the fork image's `rnas.tsv` is byte-identical to pristine upstream, so this row is the whole story.
  - **`graded_gene_knockout` (the n_tu>1 path) VERIFIED — first execution on the native tree.** `verify_overlay_variants.py` executes `multi_gene_knockout` only; graded was checked for presence and registration and never run. Three levels on **murA (n_tu=2)**, seed 0, at fixed kb `3b2f8ebd…`, run in parallel: baseline `gene_knockout` (1 of 2 TUs) leaves murA monomer at mean **1046.06 and GROWING (790 → 1403)** — the corpus's `noop_knockout` flag confirmed by direct measurement — while graded level 1 (factor 0.00, both TUs) gives **0.00, first 0, last 0**; level 5 (0.50) → 60.2%; level 7 (0.90) → 90.4%. Monotone and close to nominal. **The dose values are 1 seed × 1 generation and do not clear `support.MIN_SEEDS/MIN_GENERATIONS`; the ZERO does (0 is 0 at any seed).**

Now `threading.local()` via `_get_exec_env`/`_set_exec_env`. Pinned by three tests including one that drives two threads through a `Barrier` so both write before either reads, plus a non-vacuity test asserting `campaign` is still threaded. **Never triggered** — all three graded runs targeted murA and ran as independent containers.

## X · Capability gaps (auto-filed by the self-harness)

Written by `src/cellarium/harness.py` on every Council run: a falsifier that names a statistical test with no executable tool (see `test_registry.py`) is filed here for a developer to close. **The harness only creates `open` rows and bumps `Seen`; edit the `State` cell by hand — it is respected and never reopened.** Resolve a gap by either implementing the tool (add its `TestSpec`; the gap then stops recurring) or tightening the proposer so the Council stops naming it (set `State` to `wontfix`). Auto-filed at P3 until a dev triages; `Seen >= 3` earns a `⚑` ready-for-triage flag.

<!-- HARNESS-GAPS:BEGIN (managed by harness.py — edit only the State cell) -->

| ID | State | Seen | Missing capability | Suggested resolution |
|----|-------|------|--------------------|-----------------------|
| `GAP-7f48ca3f` | wontfix | 4× ⚑ | **hartigan_dip** named, no executable tool. We have Sarle's BC (bimodality_bc), not Hartigan's exact dip + bootstrap unimodal null. | **wontfix (2026-07-20):** the exact Hartigan dip (GCM/LCM iteration + bootstrap null) is a numerically fiddly algorithm we can't cross-validate against a reference in this env — shipping an un-validated statistic on a publication platform is the wrong risk. Instead **tightened the proposer** (`council._PROPOSER_SYS`) to use the bimodality-COEFFICIENT test we DO have and to NOT specify Hartigan's dip. Revisit only if a reviewer specifically requires the exact dip over Sarle's BC, with a validated implementation. |

<!-- HARNESS-GAPS:END -->

- **SP-2 (core) · Cellwright receptive field** (2026-07-18) — closed the silent-truncation holes, host-side and
  scipy-free (numpy). (1) `read_raw_series` swapped stride decimation for **min–max** decimation (a transient can
  no longer fall between shown points) + a `view` loss report (`extrema_in_view`, `max_abs_error_vs_full`,
  `detail_between_points` → nudge to scan). (2) New **`scan_series`** tool (`scan.py`) reads the full-resolution
  `raw.seed_channel` and returns an FDR-controlled transient/level-shift event list: robust binned-median baseline
  + MAD-prominence, gated by effect-size + min-width, with a normal-tail p × AR(1) effective-N correction and
  BH-FDR (`stats.bh_qvalues`) — deterministic, no signal-contaminated bootstrap. (3) `top_movers` gained a
  `truncation` block computing "k of N BH-significant movers dropped below the cut" from the worker's counts.
  Tests: `test_scan.py` (min–max preserves a stride-missed spike; transient vs level-shift classification; no
  false positive on clean noise; determinism; truncation block). Verified live on `wildtype/basal` (10,234
  timesteps). Deferred → **SP-2b**. pytest + ruff green.

- **SCI-1 (core) · Independent FBA cross-check over iML1515** (2026-07-18) — a second, genome-scale opinion beside
  the whole-cell sim, from the SOTA brief (`wf_2479258d`). New `src/cellarium/fba.py` (cobrapy over iML1515) +
  four tools: `fba_growth` (FBA), `fba_gene_knockout` (FBA single-deletion via the GPR + Keio-benchmark join +
  named diagnosis), `fba_flux` (pFBA point + loopless FVA range — never a bare internal flux), and
  `fba_essentiality_panel` (FBA-vs-Keio **confusion matrix + MCC**, not accuracy, + the disagreements as
  mechanistic hypotheses). Optional **`fba` extra** (keeps the core scipy-free); every tool degrades to a clear
  message when cobra/model absent; the 11 MB iML1515 SBML is fetched on demand from BiGG (gitignored).
  Reproducibility pinned in `provenance()` (model SHA-256, cobra + solver versions, medium, objective, cutoff).
  Verified live: WT growth 0.82 h⁻¹, `fbaA` → `fba_false_viable` (FBA reroutes through its isozyme; Keio-essential),
  40-gene panel MCC 0.75. Tests: pure logic (diagnosis, MCC) + gating everywhere; real FBA opt-in (skips without
  cobra/model, like `hf`), so CI is unaffected. Deferred → **SCI-1b**. pytest + ruff green.

- **SP-2b · Receptive field completion** (2026-07-18) — finished the deferred SP-2 pieces (host + worker). (1)
  **Mid-rank stratified sample**: `_reader_worker.mode_differential` now returns a stratified `mid_rank_sample` of
  the BH-significant movers dropped below the top-N cut; `differential.top_movers` annotates its symbols and
  `tools.top_movers` folds it into `truncation.mid_rank_examples` — so a real mid-rank mover is visible, not just
  counted. (2) **`scan_overview`** tool: the deterministic map-reduce — full-scans a design's channel panel in one
  call and ranks them by strongest event (no LLM fan-out; verified on `wildtype/basal`). (3) A deterministic
  **receptive-field eval** (`test_receptive_field.py`): a known needle the coarse stride view flattens is recovered
  by min-max decimation + `scan_series`, a clean trajectory yields nothing (null control), and a mid-rank mover is
  surfaced. The agent-in-the-loop graded run + the LLM-worker fan-out are deferred to **SP-2c**. pytest + ruff green.

- **SCI-1b · FBA cross-check deepening** (2026-07-18) — five additions to `fba.py`. (1) **Linear MOMA** (L1
  distance to WT flux) as the pre-adaptation comparator — runs on GLPK (quadratic MOMA needs a QP solver); where
  MOMA also stays viable the reroute is a real isozyme/pathway, not an FBA optimality artifact. (2) The **3-way
  join**: `fba_gene_knockout` + `fba_essentiality_panel` now carry the wcEcoli KO prior beside FBA + Keio (uniformly
  "viable" for metabolic genes — its documented under-prediction) plus a tally of which model catches each
  Keio-essential gene. (3) **`fba_synthetic_lethal`** — pairwise double-KO to find synthetic lethals single-deletion
  misses. (4) **`fba_sensitivity`** — growth (and a gene's essentiality call) under ±20% on medium/NGAM/GAM, so a
  conclusion is only credited if it survives the spread (fbaA growth swings 25.6%). (5) **`fba_qc`** — a MEMOTE-lite
  gate: no ATP/biomass producible with uptakes closed (energy-cycle check) + every internal reaction mass-balanced
  (excluding biomass/demand/sink + the generic polymer residue). All under the optional `fba` extra; real-FBA tests
  skip without cobra so CI is unaffected. pytest + ruff green.
  **Scoping decisions (SCI-1b remainder, 2026-07-18):** (1) **Quadratic (L2) MOMA is NOT pursued** — it needs a
  commercial QP solver (Gurobi/CPLEX + an academic license) for marginal value over the shipped linear (L1) MOMA,
  which agrees with it on the essential/viable call. (2) **MEMOTE is NOT in CI** — nothing MEMOTE runs in the
  pipeline; `fba_qc` is an in-house MEMOTE-*style* subset that runs as a tool + in the local (cobra-present) test
  suite and SKIPS in CI. A full MEMOTE scorecard would be a **separate, opt-in suite/job** (it needs
  cobra+scipy+the 11 MB model, which the main CI deliberately excludes to stay light + scipy-free) — not a change
  to the existing CI gate.

- **M-2 + LLM-3 + M-3 + DS-2 · Reproducibility & honesty bundle** (2026-07-18) — (M-2/LLM-3) sampling temperature
  is pinned instead of the API default: `agent.temperature_for(model, thinking)` returns `CELLARIUM_TEMPERATURE`
  (0.0) for models that accept an explicit temperature with thinking off, and None (omit) for reasoning models
  (opus) or when extended thinking is on (the API forces temp=1 there). Wired into `agent.converse` (both the
  normal + thinking-fallback paths) and `run_council` → `deliberate`, and recorded in the Hypothesis meta + the
  agent session. Anthropic exposes no seed, so temperature is the named variance source. (M-3)
  `provenance._is_in_sample` no longer short-circuits `wildtype` to in-sample — it gates on the condition, so
  `wildtype/acetate` is correctly out-of-sample. (DS-2) `effect_z_vs_corpus` → `z_vs_corpus_spread`, explicitly
  flagged as descriptive positioning, not significance. Tests: `test_provenance.py` + a `temperature_for` unit test,
  no regression across council/hypotheses. pytest + ruff green.

- **SCI-2 (core) · Sim-vs-real RNA-seq cross-check** (2026-07-18) — a second external oracle beside SCI-1, from the
  `wf_eeea2f6c` SOTA brief. New `src/cellarium/sci2.py` — the scientific core is a pure **log2FC concordance
  engine**: join the sim's per-gene log2FC to a DESeq2 reference on b-number, then score Pearson / Spearman /
  **Deming** slope (TLS — both axes noisy) / sign-concordance on the confidently-resolved genes, always against a
  **null baseline** (a high r is nearly free from the shared housekeeping backbone), ending in a model-limit
  verdict (CONCORDANT / DIVERGENT-model-limit / INDETERMINATE). `build_reference` runs **pydeseq2 on the DATA side
  only** (real replicates → NB-GLM Wald, **unshrunk** LFC to match the unshrunk seed-mean sim LFC); the sim side is
  the seed-mean log-ratio (seeds are NOT replicates — never fed to DESeq2, never a p-value). Optional **`rnaseq`
  extra** (pydeseq2); PRECISE-1K (~60 MB) fetched on demand + gitignored; `available()`/`provenance()` gates like
  `fba.py`. Verified: the pure engine on synthetic vectors (concordant → r 0.99, Deming slope ≈1, null ≈0;
  divergent → flagged). Tests: engine + Deming/Spearman + gating everywhere; real pydeseq2/data path is opt-in so
  CI is unaffected. End-to-end (fetch + all-gene sim reader + b-number map) → **SCI-2b**. pytest + ruff green.

- **D-1 · innerHTML escaping invariant** (2026-07-18) — hardened the SPA's XSS surface. `esc()` now escapes quotes
  too (`&quot;`/`&#39;`), closing an attribute-injection gap (a value in `class="…"`/`title="…"` could previously
  break out). Added a `safe`-tagged template helper that auto-escapes every interpolation (the go-forward pattern).
  An audit of the ~200 el()/innerHTML sinks found the named risky ones (the Council falsifier, corpus rows, the
  queue's `from_question`) already `esc()`'d; fixed the two genuine misses (a design's `genes` string, the
  clear-queue error message). Added `test_frontend_safety.py` — a CI lint that scans HTML template strings and
  FAILS on any new unescaped data interpolation (short reviewed allowlist + a not-vacuous meta-test), so the
  invariant is enforced automatically instead of hand-maintained. pytest + ruff green.

- **SCI-2b · RNA-seq cross-check, data side** (2026-07-18) — made the DESeq2 reference REAL. `fetch_precise1k()`
  pulls PRECISE-1K raw counts (17 MB, b-number-indexed) + metadata (gitignored, SHA-pinned); `build_reference`
  gained a strain-fidelity filter (default MG1655) and the correct `wt_glc` reference label. Validated end-to-end
  on a real contrast — `wt_ph5` vs `wt_glc` (19 + 4 MG1655 reps) → 4,345 genes with real acid-stress DE at padj≈0,
  unshrunk log2FC keyed by b-number. Added the committed **symbol→b-number** map (`data/cache/bnumber_map.json`,
  4,675 genes, EcoCyc-derived; pfkA→b3916 matches iML1515) and wired it into `sim_lfc` so the sim side joins the
